     - a Build tab with Place stop (a stop that stands alone until a line uses it), Draw line (today's tool, reusing placed stops) and Place depot [a placed stop is a Sign for $4,000 on the street nearest the click, refused within 250 m of a stop already standing, where the line tool would join that stop instead; it costs its upkeep and counts for nothing (coverage, contracts) until a line calls there, so EC-07's poles that scored without service cannot come back; it reads no Quiet flag while it waits; and while no line calls there it can be taken down for half its cost. Build is one dock button that opens the three tools in a menu above it, so the dock has one slot fewer];
     - the line panel shows only the line's information;
     - Edit route opens an expanded editor to reorder, add and remove stops, like Google Maps' trip planner [the editor sets the line's whole route in one command, one undo: it buys new stops and any bus the longer round trip needs, keeps the buses a shorter one leaves spare, and a stop it leaves that no other line calls at comes down for half its cost, as Remove does; Extend and Insert fold into it. A click on the map adds a stop at the end of the list, as a trip planner adds a destination, and the player drags or moves it into place; the draft's stops are numbered on the map to match the list; the card gives the route's change in length where Insert warned of a detour].
  6. **Panels and controls:**
     - panels can be moved and several used at once (the owner preferred this to one closing another) [each panel opens in a free place beside those open, the first at the top right and the next to their left, or below them when a row is full, and stays there; dragged by its heading it opens there again for the session; a press brings it to the front; a tool's card is a panel too, so a panel no longer puts the tool down; Escape closes them all; I13 measures each panel alone, as several open is the player's choice];
     - Saves leaves the bottom tabs for the top [a chip at the top right, the top bar's chips wrapping before they reach it];
     - the browser's dropdowns become custom ones [the line card's and new-line card's bus and headway menus join the speed and fare menus on the game's Dropdown, a locked bus greyed with its reason as the option's hint; `src/ui/controls.test.ts` fails if any screen draws a `<select>` again].
  7. **3D:**
     - double-click and hold to switch 2D and 3D [held still for half a second it flips; held and dragged, up tilts and down flattens; a quick double-click still zooms; only with no tool out, since a tool's first click is a real one];
     - the view rotates in 3D [dragging sideways turns it, with the right button, Ctrl or a held double-click, and so do Q and E; N or the compass faces north; 2D always faces north];
     - [the 2D | 3D chip goes and `T` stays. A compass half its size keeps a visible way into 3D, which the M5 spec never cuts, and faces the board north; the other half of its room goes to the demand menu, since I13's budget at rest is spent].
- **The clearance:** once these are built and deployed, work moves on to the schedule's next items without waiting for their calendar weeks. The first is the tuning freeze from the scripted pacing run, ahead of P1. CLAUDE.md's hard limits stand:
  - no Steam or desktop session before v1.0;
  - no renderer change after Sun Nov 1;
  - no new sim system after Sun Dec 6;
  - versions only through `/ship`.
- **Why:** the owner played the preview and named what confused or annoyed them. The owner's direction also brings the 3D and look items forward, ahead of PLAN §10's one session in three for look work before M3 is signed off.
- **How to reverse:** any item, by saying so. Each is its own pull request and can be reverted alone. The time change sets older web saves aside, and a revert does not bring them back.
- **Since amended:** 2026-10-14, the tuning freeze (Measured, below): the ladder's 4 is 2,700× (45 minutes a second, a game day in 32 s), not 1,800×, which with k 1.333 (D7, since tuned) brings the scripted player's second line on Inner Boston into PLAN §3's 15–25 minutes. 1×, 60×, 600× and 5,400× stay. The owner can move the rung back by saying so.

### D26 · Systems that work first; the real values later

- **Decided:** 2026-10-14, by the owner, while the gold measurements ran: "the money is not important right now, right now whats important is that the system work and we can fine tune the real values later".
- **What it means:**
  - Work goes to every system working end to end: no feature that fails, no screen that disagrees with the sim, no command that leaves state inconsistent, no undo or save that loses state. [First an audit of the game's twelve systems, each finding confirmed by running it and reproduced independently, then the confirmed bugs fixed in small pull requests, most severe first.]
  - Values wait: prices, rates, the pacing minutes and the medal bars are tuned later. What is merged stays (#102's Coverage fix, which was a bug; #103's k 1.333 and speed 4 at 2,700×, with their nightly bands), so the game keeps one set of numbers while the systems are fixed.
  - The gold measurement (a scripted player that builds toward the card, wider candidates and a coverage bench) is parked, not merged; the medal ladder (Open above) keeps its recommendation as the default until the values pass.
- **Why:** the owner's priority. A value tuned on a system that does not work yet is tuned against a bug, as the Coverage grade was until #102.
- **How to reverse:** the owner says the values come first again; the gold measurement picks up where it stopped.

### D27 · Fewer tokens a session: no stage a stronger model makes redundant

- **Decided:** 2026-10-17, by the owner: "can you find parts of the current workflow that is unproductive or redundant and remove or adjust them, we want to decrease the token usage", then "are there any major systems that are redundant due to the advanced nature of models? remove it if not needed".
- **What it means:**
  - **STATUS holds only what is live, waiting, in progress and next.** It had grown to 92,726 bytes (347 lines), about 60 KB of it finished work already in git, `CHANGELOG.md`, this file and the merged pull requests, and every check-in read it whole; it is now about 15 KB. Its long form stays at `git show a5c0ccd:docs/STATUS.md`, and the preview's feature list is `CHANGELOG.md`'s Unreleased, not a second copy.
  - **The session-start hook prints STATUS only at a start or a clear**, not on a resume (the conversation already holds it) or a compaction (its summary carries what is in flight): each restart of a session's worker had added another copy.
  - **A session searches this file** (318,778 bytes) for the decisions its task touches rather than reading it whole.
  - **The check-in fires every six hours, not hourly, with a fast path** (every two hours from 2026-10-17; every three from the owner's "change check ins to every 3 hours", 2026-10-19; every six from the owner's "actually every 6 hours", 2026-10-20): when `main` has not moved, no pull request is open and the next item is blocked, it fetches, answers in one line and reads nothing else, and firings queued together get one answer. Seven firings queued in P0's freeze, each asking for the whole file.
  - **No pull request only to cite a deploy:** the live check is cited in the next pull request's STATUS. #137 was a pull request, a CI run and a deploy for one line.
  - **CI is waited on through the pull request's events**, not by polling it.
  - **The audit loses its separate verifier.** One agent per finding reproduced it before the fixer reproduced it again as its failing-first test (TS-04); 16 of 157 findings were refuted, all as the recorded design or already known, which a fix checks in this file anyway. A fix sets aside, with its reason, a finding that does not reproduce or is the recorded design. Rounds still find bugs (40, 29, 18, 14 and 12 confirmed in rounds 1–5), so the audit stays, at most three lanes a round aimed at the newest code (the seventh round started six).
  - **The session fixes findings itself**, one small pull request at a time, in place of a fixer agent per batch that rebuilt context the session already had.
  - **A pull request that changes code has one independent reviewer** with both lenses (correctness, then the repo's rules and on-screen text), in place of two; a docs-only one has none. Fresh eyes still pay: the reviews of rounds 5 and 6 caught a take that blocked a Load, words untrue on screen and a cache with no bound.
  - **Nothing else runs as a workflow** (the owner, 2026-10-17: "if you think multi agent workflows are inefficient and turning them off will be the same quality of work then turn it off or at least tune it down"): docs, investigations, measurements and small changes are the session's own work. The owner's earlier "run more parallel agents" gives way to this.
- **Why:** the owner's priority. Each of these stages caught misses a session's own checks now catch, or rebuilt context a session reads from git. What guards the owner's say stays: versions only by `/ship`, the playtest freeze, the deploy's live check, TS-04's failing test and the review before a merge.
- **How to reverse:** the owner says so; the check-in goes back to hourly, workflows go back to every task, and STATUS's long form is in git.

### D28 · P0's merge freeze lifted before its notes

- **Decided:** 2026-10-21, by the owner: "skip the playtest freeze for now".
- **What it means:** merges to `main` resume without P0's notes. P0 plays V1, not this build, so a merge changes nothing it measures. P0 is not cancelled: its notes still go in `docs/playtests/2026-10-17-p0-v1-baseline.md` whenever they come. CLAUDE.md's freeze still holds for later playtests (P1, Nov 16–22) unless the owner says otherwise.

### CL · Versions only on the owner's say

- **Decided:** 2026-10-12, by the owner: "create a changelog that will only make versions with my say."
- **What it means:** `CHANGELOG.md` follows Keep a Changelog 2.0.0. Every player-visible change adds a line under Unreleased. A version exists only when the owner types `/ship <version>` ([.claude/skills/ship/SKILL.md](../.claude/skills/ship/SKILL.md), which Claude cannot start); it merges a `Release v<version>` commit through a pull request and tags that commit. `node scripts/changelog.mjs check` runs in CI as the required status check "Versions only via /ship" and fails any version not added by its own `Release v<version>` commit, any `v*` tag not on that commit, and any later edit to released notes. It cannot tell who made that commit; the CLAUDE.md rule and the owner's yes inside `/ship` are the guard. Every v1.0 date in the plan is a target for the owner's `/ship`, never automatic; milestone tags such as `prototype` never start with `v`.
- **Why:** V1's "one version per session" rule produced 24 versions in about 8 days and two sessions both cut v1.18 ([PR-15](history/lessons-ledger.md), [PR-08](history/lessons-ledger.md)).
- **How to reverse:** Remove the CI workflow and the skill. Not recommended.

## Measurements to record (weeks 1–3; the desktop from Feb 8)

Filled in as they are measured, with the date and the commit, each by a committed script ([TS-08](history/lessons-ledger.md)):
- Boston's map file size (`pmtiles extract --dry-run`); whether GitHub Pages answers Range requests with `206` and no `Content-Encoding`; and the `Cache-Control` it sends for `index.html`, hashed assets and `version.json` (D12).
- Gzip size per chunk, the menu's critical path including its font bytes (D13), and the first map frame under software GL. **First frame done 2026-10-13: 1.2–1.5 s on Inner Boston (Measured, below).**
- Whether census.gov, LEHD and Geofabrik accept downloads from GitHub's runners (measured below: they do).
- The number of Inner Boston buildings with OSM `height` or `building:levels` (week 2). Rule: under about half, the Overture height join goes into the r1 bake in week 5 and off cut 1; otherwise it is dropped. Record the count and the result here. **Done 2026-10-13: 14.1%, so the join goes in (Measured, below).**
- Sessions merged against sessions planned (PLAN §10), on Sun Oct 18, Oct 25 and Nov 1, by `npm run measure:sessions`. **Oct 18's, which went unrecorded, read from git on 2026-10-23 with week 2 so far (Measured, below).**
- Recompute time on Boston in the three-period form, total and per period (on Nov 1; R2 only if ≤100 ms p95 with three periods, D11).
- In week 9, 2D and 3D frame rates on the owner's Mac, in current stable Chrome; from Feb 8 (D18), when Electron is pinned, the same inside the packaged app, and the packaged desktop apps' sizes on disk and to download.

**Measured**
- **2026-10-29** (`npm run measure:sessions -- --today=2026-10-25` on `main` 3e5515a): **sessions merged against sessions planned, week 2** (PLAN §10's velocity check, Sun Oct 25; the count of 2026-10-23 is below). Week 2, Oct 19 to Oct 25, 6 sessions planned: 52 pull requests merged, all beyond the docs, 26 sessions at PLAN §9's two a session. The plan built still reads through week 8 (Sun Dec 6), M4's deadline: 41.5 sessions against 11.5 planned through Oct 25, 6 weeks ahead, so velocity holds (two-thirds: 7.7). None merged from Oct 26 to Oct 28, while this session's worker restarted again and again and did no work; week 3's count goes in with the first pull request after Nov 1, with the capacity check.
- **2026-10-25** (the Nightly, run 40194059048, on `main` 3e5515a; `npm run bench recompute`): **I14 on the late game on GitHub's runner, its four steps in** (the I14 question in Open, asked in the batch of 2026-10-29). On the pacing run's companies at the bus ceiling (seed 1: 14 lines on 75 stops; seed 2: 13 on 77), at p50 and p95: a headway's command 83.9–88.5 and 114.5–114.8 ms; a fare's 84.9–89.4 and 115.4–116.2; a Shelter's 53.9–56.0 and 70.6–75.4; a festival's start or end 53.2–57.8 and 71.3–77.8; a snow day's start or end 166.8–171.6 and 169.6–175.3; a whole recompute, as a load runs it, 176.0–181.5 and 179.9–185.4; the memo 30.5–33.7 MB. The late game of 30 lines with no hub: a whole recompute 139.8 and 150.7, a command 33.5 and 51.5. So every command there is under I14's 150 ms at p95; a load and a snow day's start or end, which work out nearly every cell pair afresh, are not. The nightlies of Oct 26 to Oct 28 passed on the same commit.
- **2026-10-25** (`e2e/resize.spec.ts`, `e2e/pinch.spec.ts`, `e2e/panels.spec.ts`; on `main` 3e5515a): **the systems audit's nineteenth round, its second lane: the edges of the eighteenth round's second lane's fixes, and the calendar's words** (D26; the first lane's entry comes with its fixes). It played #189's fixes on the shipped build, Inner Boston, at the owner's Mac's windows at scale 2 (1440×790, 1470×830, 1512×860, 1728×990), the artist's PC's (1920×950 at 1, 1536×730 at 1.25, 1280×590 at 1.5) and 1152×720: Chrome's zoom from 90% to 200% and back, a step at a time and in one jump, a drag of the window's corner a pixel at a time, one to six panels opened in four orders, some dragged, a pinch over 19 pieces off the map at the page's own scale and zoomed, and every place that names a day, a quarter or a year from Year 1's last days into Year 2 and from Year 5's Q4 Day 7 into Year 6 on the pacing run's company. It found 5 problems, 4 minor and 1 nit, each reproduced 3 runs of 3; only the first is new in #189. No format change.
  - **With the lender's card up, a zoom and back left a panel over another** (minor, new in #189; `src/ui/shell/Panels.tsx`). At 1152×720 with Contracts, Lines, Finance and Saves open as the run ended, the lender's card fell back over Contracts and Lines at (760, 168), as it may. After Chrome's 150% and back, Finance stood at (700, 318), 56×248 px over Contracts; after 125% and back, Saves lay 90×248 over Contracts. Back at 100% the pass reached Finance before the card, which still stood where 150% had put it, and kept Finance clear of that place; the card then went back to (760, 168), and the fresh layout, which is the opening's own, counted the card over the panels before it as two piles against the pass's one, and was turned down. Each panel is kept from where it opened now (the third finding), so the pass puts all five back as they opened. The lane's own fix, counting no pile with the card, made no difference once each panel goes back: over 4 windows with and without Help, 16 zoom steps each, the panels lay as they did with it, so the count stays as it was.
  - **A dragged panel was kept in reach of where it stood, not of where it was put** (minor, older: the rule dates from 718cd3b; `src/ui/shell/Panels.tsx`). Lines dragged to (1000, 100) and Finance to (1300, 160) at 1440×790, apart: at 150% both were kept in reach at x 912, and back at 100% both stayed there, Finance 280×244 px over Lines, a pixel either way parting nothing, as dragged panels are fixed in the fresh layout too. Finance alone, dragged to (1200, 400), stood at (912, 295) after 150% and back, though it opened again at (1200, 400). A dragged panel is kept in reach of where it was dragged to, as it is when it opens.
  - **One step of zoom and back moved the panels for good** (minor, older; `src/ui/shell/Panels.tsx`). At 1440×790, Lines, Finance and Contracts stood at x 1148, 696 and 384, and Day 2's card beside them at (12, 60). Chrome's 110% (1309×718) kept Lines whole at 1017 and placed Finance and Contracts afresh at 565 and 253; back at 100% all three stayed where they were, leaving 253 px at the left of the 292 the card needs, so every later close's card stood in the middle at (549, 60), 326×280 px over Finance's heading. A panel in a free place is kept whole from where it opened now (`homes`), so a change of size and back puts it back. It moves back as the window grows, where kept from where it stood it never moved.
  - **A page zoomed with only the map in view could not be pinched back** (minor, not covered by #189's fix; `src/ui/pinch.ts`). With the game's script held back 3 s, a pinch of 1.6 over the splash at 1440×790 left a view 900×494 px, Home's card 12% of it; founded by the mouse or the keyboard, the view was all map, and MapLibre's handler, which cancels every wheel it takes, took each pinch back: five zoomed the map from 14 to about 13.5 and the page stayed at 1.6, the chips, the dock and "© OpenStreetMap contributors" out of view (the credit at y 618 of the 494 px view); only the keyboard moved the view (6 of 6). A wheel with Ctrl that zooms out while the page is zoomed goes through over the map too, kept from MapLibre's handler: a pinch out brings the page back to 1 and, once it is there, zooms the map, so one longer than the page's zoom does both: from 1.6, a pinch of 1/3 over the map left the page at 1 and the map from 14 at 13.78 and 13.86 (the review, 2 of 2). The hold now lets a control go only as the focus leaves it for the page while the control stays there and on, as a press on text leaves it, read as the focus leaves, before anything else runs; one the page takes away or turns off, which Chrome takes the focus from as it goes, it keeps until a press (the review, below).

The editor's controls hand on now as its own presses do: a row's to the × of the row now in its place, or of the last; one turned off in its row to the row's first button still on; Save route to Cancel. Not while a save is on its way, which turns Save route off by its own press; on again, as a save turned down or taken back leaves it, Save route takes the focus back (the review).

Opened from the stops row's Stop 5, the card closed with its stop, and its opener went with it: the walk back went on to what opened Lines, the dock's Lines, while Lines stayed open, so the next Space closed Lines (3 of 3). A gone opener in a panel still open gives that panel's box, as Continue's hand-back does; a panel that stood in for another, the route editor for Lines, still walks on.

  - **A pinch zoomed the page** (major; `src/ui/pinch.ts`, new). Chrome sends a trackpad's pinch as wheel events with Ctrl held, and only MapLibre, over its own canvas, cancelled them. At 1440×790 at scale 2, a pinch of 1.6 times over Home's card or over Finance zoomed the page itself 1.6 times, the chips, the dock and "© OpenStreetMap contributors" out of view; pinching back over the same spot then fell on the map, which zoomed out while the page stayed zoomed; and Chrome kept the page's zoom through a reload. Over the map a pinch zoomed the map, as it should. One window listener now cancels a wheel with Ctrl held anywhere off the map's canvas (`onCanvas`, as the one-press guard tells the map), so a pinch there does nothing; nor does Ctrl with a mouse's wheel, which zoomed the browser over a panel and the map over the map. Ctrl and + or − and Chrome's menu still zoom the page, which the panels now stay apart through (below). Safari's gesture events are left alone: the preview is played in Chrome, and on Steam in Electron's Chromium.
  - **An event's "tomorrow" meant the day after** (minor; `src/ui/format.ts`). An event is rolled at a day's close for the next day and starts the day after that (`EVENT_WARN_DAYS`), so the map's marker reads "Roadworks tomorrow" all through the next day; but the card of the day just closed said "tomorrow" too. Day 1's card read "Roadworks on Huntington Avenue tomorrow, for 4 days", at noon on Day 2 the works were still to come and the line card's round trip as before, and they ran Days 3 to 6; snow the same, from Q2 Day 1's card to Q2 Day 3. An owner checking M4's item 2 on Day 2 saw no slower round trip.     - The late companies changed directly, 308: what no one command makes, such as a festival with a headway change or a Shelter at once, two Shelters at once, a line left with no buses and given them back, and the lines reordered.
    - The world's assignment between commands and after time ran, 1,868 times.

    - Pre-#179 `main`'s assignment, run beside it on 382 of them, gave the same numbers.

  - **Memory and time.** Over the long session the heap and typed arrays held at 67.5–85.8 MB (68.5 at the start, 69.6 at the end); the arenas grow and shrink with the network (16.9 to 27.5 to 11.0 MB on seed 1), the kept tables hold 0.1–0.4 MB, the plan-transition memo stays within its caps (1.5–1.7 MB), and `commutersToday`'s cache holds one 4.3 MB festival a world. No command lets the memo go, and timed both ways on 382 recomputes the memo was at or under pre-#179's whole assignment, at p50 and p95, for every kind.

The bench now also holds both companies after a run of snow days' starts and ends and after each of 6 more, and through 6 model swaps, 6 stops made Stations, 6 lines' second stops taken off and 6 lines left with no buses, each undone by the next. A check that finds a difference runs its steps out, so the company is as it was for the next kind (the review). A stop's distances change only with the stops, which the tables are told by too, so no change in the game reaches that slip.

The bench now times it beside a festival's, and holds it to a whole assignment: on this shared machine, with the second lane's browser running, 100 runs a case, a snow day's start or end takes 229.0–246.7 ms at p50 and 307.0–348.9 at p95, beside 252.7–277.2 and 304.0–389.1 for a whole recompute in the same run. So two closes a snow day wait as a load does, and I14's open item says so (Open: I14 on a hub of lines).

Each command taken while paused keeps the game from before it (`src/sim/undo.ts`), 71.0–77.2 kB on the late companies (a save of 69 kB as text, with 981 rows of the ledger) against 3.2 kB on a fixture game of one line, all let go on Play: 500 commands while paused hold 35.5–38.6 MB. The fourteenth round's 300 commands, 10.2 to 12.6 MB, were a smaller game's. The M3 spec (item 7) keeps every command since the last unpause, newest first, so there is no cap.

A command on Line 1, 3,975 fresh pairs at 67–71 ms, spent about half its time carrying the kept rides again; one on Line 10, 51,935 fresh pairs at about 165 ms, about three quarters on its fresh pairs: finding each pair's ways to ride 17%, its sub-points' sets 12% and scanning them 21% of the profile, with carrying 9% and the lines' services 4%. So the busiest lines' commands, the p95's, gain little from carrying more. - **2026-10-24** (`src/sim/demand/memo.test.ts`; `npm run bench recompute`; on `main` da380ce): **I14 on the late game, its fourth step: a Sign made a Shelter works out only the cell pairs near it** (Open: I14 on a hub of lines, (b)). No format change, and no sim number moves.
  - **What it does.** A stop's tier reaches each line through it as the stop's walk bonus and its change penalty. A Sign made a Shelter, or back, changes only the walk bonus (0 to 0.8 minutes; the penalty is 6 at both), and a walk bonus reaches a cell pair's rides only through the walk from its home end to the line it rides first, or from the line it rides to work to its work end. So a line whose plan changed only in its stops' walk bonuses (`NOT_WALKED`) marks only the pairs with an end whose groups on that line walk to a changed stop (`markNear`), not every pair it has a route in; the catchments are still worked out whole. The memo tells such a line by copies of what the last assignment read, the rest of each plan as text and its walk bonuses (`walkedOf`), not by the plans, which a caller could change in place. A Station and up change the penalty too, which reaches every pair that changes lines at the stop, near it or not, so they still mark every pair of their lines' routes.

- **2026-10-24** (`src/sim/demand/memo.test.ts`; `npm run bench recompute`; on `main` 67fb7e0): **I14 on the late game, its second step: the catchments and the ride tables kept between commands, and the kept rides carried word by word** (Open: I14 on a hub of lines, (b)). No format change, and no sim number moves.
  - **What it does.**
    - The catchments, who can walk to which stops, depend only on where each line's stops stand and their tiers: while those and the lines are as they were, in the same order, the last assignment's serve again (`reachSignature`). A headway, a fare, a model or a line's rush leaves them; a stop moved, added or retiered, or a line drawn or closed, works them all out afresh.
    - Each line's ride tables, and each change's, are kept by what they are made from (`tableSignature`), those the last assignment used, so a command works out afresh only the changed line's and its changes'.
    - Both signatures are the whole plan but the fields named as not read, as `lineSignature` is since #179's review, so a field a plan gains costs only speed.
    - The kept rides are copied word by word as they are carried again, and a clean home cell's pairs at once: a session profile (indicative, not kept) had each pair's few rides copied through two new views (`subarray` and `set`) costing about a twentieth of a command.

The next marks the pairs a changed line has a route in, by the last assignment's catchments and by its own, since a route gone or come is in one of them; it works those out afresh and carries every other ride again in its place and order, a home cell with none of them whole. Every ride is added in the same order, so the riders are a whole assignment's to the last bit: `memo.test.ts` holds them number for number (Object.is) on the fixture through a headway, a fare, a model's speed, a stop moved, a stop's tier, the distances along a line and a change's penalty each alone, rush, a line gone and back, a line added, two lines in the other order and a festival's commuters, and `npm run bench recompute` holds the pacing run's late companies to a whole assignment after 103 commands each. A reload, which assigns from nothing, finds the same riders (I8). A load, a festival's start and end (other commuters), the fare band (`only`, with its catchments given) and lines in another order, which nothing in the game makes, assign from nothing.

  - **Continue's hand-back** (minor; `src/ui/game/DayCard.tsx`, `src/ui/focusBack.ts`, `src/ui/giveFocus.ts`). Continue handed the keyboard's focus to the page three ways, each from the busy quarter's close with Contracts open from the keyboard: from the row a close put in a withdrawn offer's place (new in #175), from the row Take it gave the focus to (older), and from a Take it the next close withdrew, the card kept across the two closes (new in #175). A row takes the focus only until it leaves, as Panels' box does, so the row had let its tabindex go as the focus went to Continue, and a Take it gone could take nothing. The hand-back now makes what it hands to focusable again (`giveFocus`, Panels' own, shared now), and where the focus came from, gone, Continue gives it to the panel that held it, or, that closed too, to what opens it. The card often shows a render before the snapshot that withdraws the offer (the host sends one every 100 ms at most), so the row takes the focus after the card has shown; the card follows that hand-off, made in the commit that takes the place away. With the following taken out, Continue gave Contracts' box, 4 runs of 4 at 60×; at 600× the two often render together, so one test holds each answer apart. Since its review, Help over the card counts as the card: opened from Continue as the next close came, Help was taken for where the focus came from, and gone as Continue handed the focus back, so the focus went to the page (on `main` too). A close while the focus is in the panel it went to straight from the card leaves where it came from as it was, and the card follows a place taken away only with the focus on it, as the hand-off it follows is made.
  - **"Stay above -$150k tonight"** (minor; `src/sim/step.ts` `cashAtClose`, `src/ui/shell/TopBar.tsx`). Four closes behind and $50 above the floor at 23:50 on the debug line, the chip said to stay above tonight. The close banked the last hour (fares +$6.75, subsidy +$4.80, running −$17.46, wages −$42.31, deadhead −$6.88, upkeep −$12.50, stop upkeep −$0.67) and charged $90.01 of interest before it counted, $158.28 in all, so it left −$150,108.28 and the run ended; $150 above went the same way, and $200 above stayed above. The snapshot's countdown now carries the cash the coming close is set to count (`tonightCents`), if the day runs on as it is set: the hours left banked and checked against the held contracts as they stand, then the close's money as `simStep` posts it (`closeMoney`, one function for both): a contract's reward, the interest on what is owed after it, and on a quarter's last day the card's grant or fine. It works on a copy of the lines, the contracts and the ledger. Where that is below the floor the chip says "Heading below -$150k tonight". Its unit tests hold it to the close's cash to the cent from 00:00, 09:30, 23:00 and 23:50, and on the nights a held contract pays, a quarter's last among them. One interest rule (`interestCents`) serves the close, Finance's forecast and this, which is worked out again only as a row posts, the hour turns or the network changes; a contract taken in between has no day met yet, so it pays nothing that night.
    - **Since its review:** the forecast first counted the hours and the interest alone, so on a night a held contract paid $60,000, $50 above the floor at 23:50 and four closes behind, the chip said "Heading below -$150k tonight" (−$150,148.72) as the close came to −$90,112.72 and cleared the count, and a player might have sold buses at half price on it. On a quarter's last day a fine ($6,400) could take a company below under "Stay above -$150k tonight", as round 14's review found with no lines left and $5,000 above.

Three of them (the dots, the buildings and Export's name) come from code older than #165. No format change.
  - **Two lines resting on a street they share** (minor; `src/render/map/overlays.ts`, RESTING). Each resting copy laid its lines at RESTING_OPACITY over the streets, so two resting on a street they share blended, the later over the earlier. On Huntington Avenue at 02:00, Line 2's green over Line 1's blue drew (96, 146, 133): 0.058 in OKLab from Line 2's own faded colour, (129, 159, 134), and 0.070 from Line 1's. Every new line rests at night, overnight off.
    - **The fix.** The route's and the stops' copies are opaque now: each colour is RESTING_OPACITY of the line's own over the resting ground, so the later line hides the earlier, as by day, on a street and at a stop. The names and the flags, which never lie over one another, keep their opacity.
    - **The ground** is the main roads' colour at the darkest step, the roads lines run on (`restingGround`: #b6aea7 in the warm set, #baa183 in V1's paper, each its arterials' at night). It is a game token, the same at every step, so the light's steps lay out nothing for it. At dawn, while a line still rests, the map mixes the copies with the step's main roads instead (`loadMap.ts`, showGround), at most four times a night: at 03:30, 04:00 and 04:30, and back at 05:00 with the filters that wake the lines. A colour of each feature's, set anew, lays out the lines' source again and takes no fade, so for the 1.5 s of a dawn step's fade the resting line is a step ahead of the roads around it.
    - **One line resting alone** on a main road looks as it did laid over it: Line 2's green is (129, 159, 134) there. On a minor street it stands farther from the street than before. The nearest of the eight colours is 0.135 in OKLab from it, where laid over it the nearest was 0.097.
    - **The stops** of a resting line lie under the running lines' routes, and under the lines' names.
    - **Not taken: MapLibre 6.11.2's `line-layer-opacity`.** It fades a layer whole, drawn offscreen and then laid over the map, and it drew Line 2's own (129, 159, 134) on all 100 of the box's line pixels. But three things ruled it out:
      - its compositing program linked at the first rest, in 181 ms on software GL, and the night's test forbids any link;
      - it ran its offscreen pass at every frame while under 1;
      - its offscreen picture has no depth, so in 3D resting lines would show through the buildings that hide the running ones.
    - **Not taken: a fixed mix with the night's streets.** It left the faded line darker than the lightening streets at dawn, by up to (34, 33, 24) at 04:30.
  - **The dots counted after every frame** (minor; `loadMap.ts` dotsDrawn, `MapView.tsx`). MapLibre fires idle after any frame that leaves nothing to load, and while time runs the buses ask for frame after frame, so the map counted the dots it drew after each. On Inner Boston that was 57–70 counts in 10 s in Low graphics, 15.4–17.4 ms each and 78–79% of the render's JavaScript, and 17–21 in 10 s in 3D.
    - The map now counts at the first settle after the camera, the map's size, the dots, the kinds shown or the style changes.
    - It counts only with `?counts` (src/platform's countsSwitch). The count feeds only `data-dots`, for the e2e tests (TS-07), so a player's page counts nothing.
  - **The buildings counted at each tilt** (minor; `MapView.tsx`). The buildings in view were counted as each tilt settled, for `data-buildings` and `data-measured`, which only the tilt tests read: 130–217 ms of a 202–313 ms long task. This count also runs only with `?counts` now.
  - **Street names in the game's sentences** (minor; `src/ui/format.ts`, streetName). The city's names are OpenStreetMap's, and 25 of Inner Boston's 3,535 broke the copy convention in the day card's roadworks and festival news and on the line card:
    - 18 with a straight apostrophe, among them Monsignor O'Brien Highway (18 edges);
    - 6 route numbers in capitals: US 1, MA 2, MA 2A, MA 3, MA 16 and MA 28;
    - one street's initials, RC Kelley Street.

    The sentences now take a curly apostrophe and the route's number as Boston says it (Route 2A, Route 1), and RC joins the copy rule's capitals that are names. A unit test runs every Inner Boston name through the sentences where the city file is present; CI fetches it.

    - **V1's roads by class:** motorways in its louder amber, primary and trunk roads in the arterials' amber, secondary and tertiary roads near white, minor streets white. The generator now draws a class of major road in layers of its own where a set colours it apart (`byClass` in `style.ts`); the warm set colours none apart. Its review found that in one colour, the arterials' amber had taken the secondary and tertiary roads, 57.5% of the major roads' length in the play area, which V1 drew near white.
    - **V1's names:** every street's in its dark ink (the review: minor streets' names had taken its neighbourhoods' tan), neighbourhoods' in its tan, water's in its blue.
    - **What V1 did not draw:** hospitals, schools and airfields were its land. Paths, piers and runways take its tertiary roads' fill; V1 drew runways as grey lines, so Logan's runways read paler than V1's. Building outlines and boundaries take its streets' casing.

- **2026-10-23** (`src/render/map/daylight.test.ts`, `src/render/map/overlays.test.ts`, `src/render/map/loadMap.test.ts`, `src/render/map/fleet.test.ts`, `src/sim/rules/position.test.ts`, `e2e/daylight.spec.ts`; on `main` 4464d22): **the systems audit's eleventh round, its first lane: the day and night tint** (D26, D13). Two finder lanes ran on the code merged since the tenth round: the tint and the time of day (#168), and the edges of the tenth round's fixes (#166, #167). The first found 5 bugs, all minor or nits; the second found 9, with their own entry and pull request. No format change.
  - **A resting line on a street it shares.** Lines on the same streets draw over one another, the later on top, and the fade was an opacity of each line's id. Where the resting line was the later, the running line under it showed (34, 120, 132): 0.35 of the resting green over 0.65 of the running blue, neither line's colour. At the middle of Huntington Avenue, 168 of its pixels blended and none showed the running blue.
    - The five layers a resting line fades in are now drawn twice. Under each layer sits a faded copy (`<layer>-resting`) that holds the resting lines' features at 35%, its other paints the layer's own; the layer itself holds the rest. The fade moves features between the two by filter alone (`setResting` in `src/render/map/overlays.ts`).
    - Now 168 pixels show the running blue and none blend.
    - MapLibre places the upper layer's names first, so a resting line's name is still placed after the running lines', as the tint's review asked, with no sort key.
  - **The first night's hold.**
    - **The cause:** the faded paints went from a number to an expression of each line's id. MapLibre compiles its line, symbol and circle programs for an opacity that depends on the feature the first time it draws one.
    - **What it cost:** at the first midnight of a session, on the busy scene's 14 lines and 64 buses, one task of 518–1,249 ms in Low graphics, 786–1,528 ms in full 2D and 1,710–1,849 ms in 3D. A CPU profile put 442 ms in getUniformBlockIndex and 371 ms in getProgramParameter. Later midnights cost nothing, the programs being kept.
    - **The fix:** a constant opacity is a uniform whatever its value (MapLibre 6.11.2's `ProgramConfiguration` keys a program by which paints are constant, not by their values), so each copy draws with its layer's own program.
    - **Programs linked:** the night test counts them as the line rests: none (14 before and after), and 17 with the opacity of each line's id put back.
    - **Long tasks after the fix:** on the busy scene in Low graphics the night's rests took none over 200 ms in two runs, and none at all in a third. In 3D in full graphics they took one of 157–170 ms, as did the same night with no line resting (163 ms, and none).
    - **What remains:** a rest still lays out the lines' tiles again, as before.
  - **Resting once its last bus is back.**
    - **Before:** the fade followed the period alone. From 00:00 a line showed faded while its buses ran its last trips: 4 of the debug line's buses at every 5 minutes at 00:00, the last until 00:17, and 16–18 of the busy scene's 64 at 00:10. D13 fades a line "while it is stopped".
    - **The rule now:** a line rests once its period runs no service and no bus of it is in service: one predicate still, `restsAt` in `src/sim/rules/periods.ts`, given whether a bus is in service by `inServiceAt` in `src/sim/rules/position.ts`. A bus is in service from its first departure until its last trip is back at the first stop, on the plan the costs bill, and on a settling line's merged plan, read in its frame.
    - **On the busy scene:** by their plans its 14 lines rest from 00:06:00 to 00:15:40, in five to seven steps as the snapshots come at 600×, about a game minute apart; the chip read 00:00 to 00:10 through them, its minutes floored to tens at that speed (its review). A rest comes at most a snapshot after the last bus is back, and never before.
    - **Depot drives:** a bus driving home to its depot draws on a faded line, as one pulling out before 05:00 does.
  - **The flags.** A flag is a stop's, so the fade of each line's features never reached it, and a resting line's Loss rings and words stayed whole at 02:00. A stop's flag now rests where only resting lines call (`restingStops`); a stop a running line calls at keeps its flag whole.
  - **The light's plan:** 93 paints a step (91 before); the two more are the resting names' inks. The game's layers are 23 (18 before).
  - **Checked and sound by the lane:**
    - a dusk at 5,400× on the busy scene in Low graphics, full 2D and 3D: four steps each, with no long task and no tile laid out again for the city, the dots or the lines;
    - every paint with one owner;
    - the chip's period agreeing with its time at every speed;
    - I13 with Overnight, the widest of the names (47.9 px): 3.13%, 3.86% and 4.89% at the three sizes;
    - a night's session replaying to the same game.
  - **A comment:** the night test's comment said the day card waits with time paused, but nothing pauses at a close. The test now starts after midnight, with no day card.
  - **Tests** (TS-04):
    - **Unit, each failing on 4464d22:**
      - a line rests once no bus of it is in service (failing too with the rest by period alone);
      - the faded copies, each just under its layer, the same but for its opacities (no copy);
      - `setResting` by filters alone (`setPaintProperty` called);
      - a flag rests where only resting lines call;
      - the fleet reads a settling line in its frame, failing with the day's own seconds.
    - **Unit, new rules:** `inServiceAt` on a day plan and on a merged plan; MapLibre's style spec validates the filters with lines resting and without.
    - **e2e** (`e2e/daylight.spec.ts`):
      - the night, at 00:00:30, failing on 4464d22 (already resting) and with the per-line opacity put back (17 programs against 14);
      - the shared street, about 7 s, failing on 4464d22 (no pixel in the running blue).
- **2026-10-23** (`src/render/map/daylight.test.ts`, `src/render/map/style.test.ts`, `src/render/map/loadMap.test.ts`, `e2e/daylight.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`, `e2e/time.spec.ts`): **the day and night tint** (PLAN §4's charm moments, §8 rule 8; D13), after V1's (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/map/MapView.tsx:341-413`), with the two reads D13 puts beside it. It is look work: the first since the stamp (#165), with round 10's two pull requests between. No format change.
  - **The light** (`src/render/map/daylight.ts`).
    - **When it changes:** the map dims a step each half hour from 19:00, as the evening peak ends, and is night from 20:30 to 03:30. It lightens a step each half hour from 03:30, to day at 05:00, as the service day opens and a new game starts (`darkStep`).
    - **Why dawn ends at 05:00:** dawn first ran from 05:00 to 06:30, so a new game opened on a map three quarters of the way to night. Home, the founding card and the first line all stood on it (its review).
    - **How it changes:** eight changes a game day, each set once. MapLibre's 1.5 s transition carries each step. A step comes at once with less motion asked for, as the style loads, and as the style comes back after a lost context. V1 blended its tint every 1.5 s of real time.
    - **One generator:** it builds every step from the light tokens dimmed. At night each colour keeps 0.5, 0.52 and 0.625 of its red, green and blue in linear light, a cool dusk (`NIGHT_LIGHT`).
    - **What a step sets:** 91 paints: 83 of the board's, on 71 of its 72 layers, and 8 of the game's. The game's are its lines' names, in the labels' inks, and the opacities of its translucent marks. The game's colours (lines, buses, dots, depots and flags) stay as they are.
    - **The night colours:** land #adadb5, streets #bcbfcf and water #859ebc.
    - **The parks:** their colour was a `case` on each park's kind, and a colour that depends on each feature changes only by laying out again every tile of its source, the 3D buildings' too. Its review measured a step re-laying out 12 tiles in 2D and 18 in 3D, with a 612 ms task in 3D on software GL; the parks' paint alone took 294–377 ms. Such a colour also takes no transition, so the parks drew a step ahead of the land round them. The generator now splits such a fill into one layer for each colour it picks, each colour constant (`byColour` in `style.ts`), and no paint the light changes depends on a feature.
  - **The game's marks at night:**
    - **Opaque marks:** every one keeps at least 0.15 apart in OKLab from each base colour it is drawn over: 0.159 at night (the Amber line on a building) against 0.278 by day.
    - **Translucent marks,** as drawn: none comes nearer the base under it at any step than the nearest does by day, which is the depot land's tint on a building, at 0.092. At their day opacities the tint fell to 0.062 at night, a home dot to 0.124 and a job's ring over water to 0.103 (its review). So they grow more opaque as the map dims, up to 1.6 times by night (`louder`), capped at 1:
      - depot land 0.28 → 0.448;
      - homes 0.55 → 0.88;
      - jobs' rings 0.7 → 1;
      - roadworks ahead 0.6 → 0.96, and on 0.7 → 1;
      - the walk rings 0.08 → 0.128.
    - **The warning words** (QUIET, LOSS, roadworks ahead) and the line being drawn's numbers now keep a halo of their own (`wordHalo`), as readable at every step as by day: 4.20:1 for the warning ink. In the labels' halo, which dims while the warning ink does not, they fell to 2.32:1 at night (its review).
    - **The labels:** 4.70:1 on their halos (5.82 by day), and the minor ones 3.32:1 (3.76).
  - **What it costs to go darker:** a filter at 0.9 of this one, [0.45, 0.468, 0.5625], fails three of these: 0.147 apart, the depot tint nearer than by day, and 4.49:1.
  - **A line resting** (`restsAt` in `sim/rules/periods.ts`, D13's one predicate): a line that runs no service in the period. So a line with overnight off shows its route, name and stops at 35% from 00:00 to 05:00, set as the set of resting lines changes. Its name is placed after the running lines': faded alone, it kept its place on a shared street, and the running line had no name there (its review). Since amended (the eleventh round, above): it rests once no bus of it is in service, from its last bus's return after 00:00, with the flags of the stops only it serves, and draws in faded copies under the running lines.
  - **The clock names the period** after the time: Peak, Off-peak or Overnight, the period of the minute it shows, so the two never disagree as it shows whole hours above 600×.
    - **Without room:** late in a game, with three medals and "3 on offer", the chip row wrapped to two rows (10.09% of 1152×720 on Inner Boston's numbers, against I13's 6%), and the fixture's last chip was cut (5.98%).
    - **With the year moved off the chip:** the chip reads "Q1 Day 1, 02:00 Overnight", with the year in its title ("Year 10, Q1, Day 1") and read to a screen reader, which reads no title. All ten of I13's cases pass, the late game's at 02:00, so the chip shows Overnight, the widest of the three names.
    - **Elsewhere:** the year stays on the day card, the report card, Home and Saves. Whether the year should stay on the chip instead goes to the owner in the batch of Mon Oct 26.
  - **Tests:**
    - **The e2e dusk**, from 18:55 at 600×: four steps and four sets of the paints, while the map drew 39 frames (software GL, Low graphics), and no error logged. The fade is 1.5 s.
    - **The e2e night,** as one test, which saves about 20 s of the first quarter: Home is in daylight before founding; with less motion asked for, the line rests at 00:00 and the clock reads Overnight; the light comes a step lighter at once and the line is whole by 05:00; Peak comes at 06:30.

      - the light waits for the style and comes at once with it, and fails with that line taken out;

      - day comes at once after a lost context.
- **2026-10-23** (`e2e/keyboard.spec.ts`, `e2e/start.spec.ts`, `e2e/floor.spec.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Panels.test.ts`, `src/render/map/tilt.test.ts`): **the tenth round's other eight fixes** (D26; the round's entry below), all from the second lane, where the keyboard's focus fell to the page or a key went past an open list. No format change.
  - **The hand-back as a panel closes** (`src/ui/shell/Panels.tsx`, handBackTo). A panel the keyboard opened kept what opened it only while open, went on only through panels closing at the same moment, and recorded a new opener when asked for again while open. So:
    - the route editor, which takes Lines' place and gives it back with its Cancel or Save route, handed back to nothing: Escape from it, or from Lines after its Cancel, gave the page the focus (major);
    - a stop card's line button made itself Lines' opener, and Escape went round from Lines to the stop card and back to a gone button;
    - the checklist's last step, which opens Contracts, went as Take it completed it, and Escape gave the page the focus (major: the M3 exit check's path).

    Now an opener is kept past its panel's close and let go when the panel opens again without the keyboard; a panel that took the focus from one going as it opened records that panel; one asked for again while open keeps its opener; the walk never goes round a panel twice; and with every opener gone, what opens the panel now (`data-opens`: the dock's Lines and Finance, the Contracts chip and the checklist's step, the Saves chip) takes the focus.

    The pull request's first CI run caught one more (`e2e/floor.spec.ts`): as the run's end took down a route editor the keyboard had opened, its hand-back went on through Lines, which it stood in for, to the dock's Lines, before the closing day's Continue, which takes the focus at the run's end, found it lost. Continue takes it first now (`DayCard`, a layout effect, ahead of the panels' hand-back).
  - **Back to Home** from Saves or the founding card took its button with the game's screen, and the next Tab went to the map's credits, Home's Continue the sixth (major). Home's first button takes the focus now when the keyboard pressed Back to Home, never on the page's first Home; not after the mouse's, where Space would then press Continue.

At §9's rate they would make 68.5 sessions in week 1 and 12 in week 2 so far, an overstatement, so the verdict rests on the plan built instead. Week 2's full count goes in with Mon Oct 26's question batch, and week 3's with the next pull request after Nov 1, each read after a `git fetch` with `--today` set to that Sunday.

- **2026-10-22** (`npm run bench lategame` on the three companies its late-game measurement kept, below, now summing each one's ledger from founding; on `main` 9cc5c4a with this pull request's bench): **where a company's money comes from and goes** (D26's values pass: the running costs and the city's money).
  - **The profit-only company** (seed 1, to day 201):
    - it took in $1,969,348 in fares and the city's subsidy (fares $1,068,708, the subsidy $900,640) and $2,337,126 in rewards (contracts $1,950,000, card grants $387,126);
    - it spent $1,282,719 running (wages $815,816, the cost a kilometre $330,277, depot upkeep $107,338, stop upkeep $14,949, dead-heading $14,340) and $2,280,000 buying (buses $1,425,000, depots $715,000, stops $140,000), from $474,548 at founding.

    Its fares and the subsidy cover its running 1.5 times, its fares alone 0.83 times: the subsidy and the card grants ($1,287,766) about pay the running costs. Rewards are 54% of all it took in.
  - **The card-building companies** (seed 1 to day 145, seed 2 to day 152): fares and the subsidy $3,409,078 and $4,701,272, three times their running ($1,136,830 and $1,467,874), and fares alone 1.65 and 1.75 times; rewards $2,838,737 and $2,739,434, 45% and 37% of all they took in; and $5,130,000 and $5,138,000 bought, $1,980,000 of it the five depots.
  - **Running costs** fall the same way in all three: wages 61–64%, the cost a kilometre 26–30%, depot upkeep 7–8%, stop upkeep and dead-heading about 1% each.
  - **What it means.** The running costs' rates (all but stop upkeep, D25's 1‰ a day), the fare, the subsidy and the card's payout are V1's (PLAN §3: prices stay V1's, and `calib.tripScale` scales demand), and nothing here asks to move them. A network that reaches the dense neighbourhoods earns three times its running costs in fares and the subsidy, and one on the corridors alone one and a half times, where its fares alone fall short. What tips the balance is the rewards, mostly contracts, whose $60,000–240,000 are V2's (`Intralines_City_dep@archive/intralines-city-v1:Gaming/src/10-core.js:289`), so they go with the question on what a contract asks (STATUS, In progress). The rates stay.
- **2026-10-22** (`npm run bench pacing` on seed 1 with `PACING_REPORT_ONLY=1`: the profit-only player to day 200, and the card-building player, `PACING_CARD_CENTS=16000 PACING_CANDIDATES=wide`, to gold or day 200, each with and without `PACING_CONTRACTS=off`; `npm run bench fares` and `npm run bench crowding`; on `main` a842dcb with this pull request's switch, but for the card-building player's run with contracts, which is the gold measurement's below, on 37965a4 with #144's harness, `src/sim` having changed only in comments since): **what the contracts carry, and the fare band** (D26's values pass; the M5 spec, item 2's tunables; GD-04).
  - **What the scripted player does with contracts.** It takes every offer at each close and never builds for one. On the profit-only run it took 69 in 200 days, each on the day it was dealt: 50 lapsed, 3 were still held at the end, and the 16 its lines met paid $1,950,000: 13 HEADWAY (three every 10 minutes at $240,000, five every 15 at $150,000 and five every 20 at $60,000) and 3 RIDERSHIP at 150 boardings ($60,000). That is as much as all its fares and subsidy over the run ($1,947,303). The first paid on day 54.
  - **Without them** (`PACING_CONTRACTS=off`) both players meet the early targets on the same days: profit on day 1 (5.4 minutes), the card on day 8, the second line on day 33 (23.4 minutes), bronze on day 8 and silver on day 29. After that the contracts pay for the growth:
    - the profit-only player draws its third line on day 81 instead of 55 and its fourth on day 161 instead of 102, and ends day 200 with 4 lines, 8 buses, Coverage 18.1 and $202,086, against 7 lines, 15 buses, Coverage 31.0 and $1,207,664;
    - the card-building player has no gold by day 200 (129.5 minutes): it stands at 78.9, Coverage 55.5, with 12 lines on 24 buses in 4 depots, where with contracts it got gold on day 144 (103.2 minutes) on 30.
  - **What it means.** GD-04 keeps contracts as the goal layer and the early game's cash channel. Here none pays before day 54, after the early targets, and the player changes nothing for them: most of the 50 that lapsed asked for service its network lacked (no RIDERSHIP ask of 400 or 800 ever paid), and the 16 that paid were paid for service its lines happened to run. That pays for three of the six lines the profit-only player draws after its first and for most of its late cash ($1.2 million, the late game's measurement next). Whether an offer should ask for service its landmark does not have yet goes to the owner with next week's questions (STATUS, In progress).
  - **The fare band** (`FARE_BAND_SHARE`, the fares within 5% of the best fare's net; `npm run bench fares`, Inner Boston's ten corridor lines on Sparrows every 10 and 15 minutes). On the lines that earn, the band holds one or two fares, $1.75 and $2.00, around a best fare of $1.75 or $2.00. That is under the $2.25 reference, at or below which a paid boarding brings the whole $1.60 subsidy. On East Boston, which loses about $2,600 a day at its best fare, every fare from $1.00 to $5.00 nets within 5% of that, so all are in the band, which tells the player the fare does not matter there. The share stays.
  - **Crowding** stays (K27; `npm run bench crowding`). At calibrated demand, Inner Boston's ten corridor lines turn away nobody on Sparrows or Metro 40s every 10 and 15 minutes (8,407 riders a day on Sparrows). On Sparrows every 20 minutes, Washington Street turns away 127 a day at 17:00, 2.1% of the ten lines' 5,891. The profit-only company turns away none of its 4,676, and the card-building company at the 30-bus ceiling 4.7–4.8% (the late game's measurement, next). So full buses turn riders away only on the busiest corridor at a long headway, and at the ceiling.

Each company is measured on its first day with no event on, after playing on as it stands through any that is: seed 1's profit-only company through a festival on day 201, whose riders more than doubled that day's net, and seed 1's card-building company through the last day of roadworks, day 145. On that day, every stop's next tier, every line's bigger bus (at its headways, and less often, at the longest steps that keep its seats an hour) and every line a step more often are each played for a day and taken back. Each kind's best per stop or line that pays alone is then played all together.
  - **The profit-only company** (seed 1, day 202): 7 lines, 15 buses in 3 depots and 35 stops, with $1,218,303 and $4,182 a day more.
    - Its buses never fill: they turn away none of 4,676 riders a day. So no bigger bus pays at its headways (the 14 cost $9,075,000), and run less often the two best together cost $330,000, bring $781 a day (423 days) and free two bus places.
    - 34 of 35 Shelters pay alone; together they cost $408,000 and bring $1,835 a day (222 days; the best alone 57).
    - Two of its seven lines pay a step more often, both on spare buses, $355 a day together for nothing. Two more on spare buses lose, and the three that need a bus do not pay.
    - So about a third of its cash has a use that pays back, in 222 days. The ceiling never binds: it stops at 15 buses because no corridor line scores.

Full buses turn away 4.7–4.8% of their riders (1,292 a day of 27,078 and of 27,424), up to 729 on one line.
    - Shelters: on seed 2 all 77 together cost $924,000 and bring $7,221 a day (128 days; the best alone 24). On seed 1, 74 of 75 pay alone (the best in 18 days), more than its cash and credit buy at once.
    - Bigger buses at their headways: seed 2 can afford every one, and 3 of 21 pay alone. The best is on its most crowded line (590 turned away): Metro 40s for $637,500, which bring $1,126 a day, 566 days. On seed 1, 2 of 11 tried pay, in 3,510 days or more, and 10 more cost more than its cash and credit. Seven on seed 1 and five on seed 2 need buses the full depots cannot park: a bigger bus is slower, so the same headways need more of them.
    - Bigger buses less often, the one way to free a bus place: 2 of 28 pay on seed 1 and 1 of 26 on seed 2. The best on each is a line's two Sparrows for one Metro 40 every 45 minutes: $165,000, for $178 and $100 a day (926 and 1,658 days) and one bus place. Of the rest, only one loses less than $850 a day, and added up, they take 9–11 points off the card.
    - A step more often: 10 lines on seed 1 and 11 on seed 2 have no room. Four lines on seed 1 and two on seed 2 can run a step more often on spare buses, for nothing: all four on seed 1 pay, $2,528 a day together, and one on seed 2, $1,457.
    - The trials add calls at stops only by running more often, which the full depots refuse, so none brings seed 2's Coverage (58.6) to gold's 60. Redrawing a line toward residents not yet in reach (Route), which can add Coverage on the same buses, was not tried.
  - **Where the ceiling binds.** At 30 buses a company can redraw a line elsewhere (Route), but has no plain way to move buses from one line to another. A line run less often, or redrawn shorter, parks its spare buses in their depot places, and only a model change sells a line's buses, buying what its headways need of the new model. No command closes a line or sells a spare bus. V1 could do both (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/game/store.ts:1391` and `:1461`), and its buses belonged to the company, not to a line, so a closed line's buses waited for another line to take them for nothing (`:1366-1384`). No decision dropped either.
  - **What it means for the values.** No price makes a bigger bus the late game's purchase. It can win back only the riders full buses turn away: on seed 2's most crowded line, 590 a day, at most $2,124 a day at $3.60 a paid boarding (its $2.00 fare and the $1.60 subsidy), of which the swap wins $1,126. For that swap to pay back within 100 days, a Metro 40 would have to cost no more than about $85,000, less than a Sparrow. So the prices stay, and the cash piles up: Shelters, about $900,000 paying back in about 130 days, are a company's one good purchase at the ceiling. What the ceiling takes away is the moves: under it a bus goes from one line to another only through a model change, which pays back in 926 days at best. The Open row above asks which verbs to add.

- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/versions.test.ts`; `npm run bench versions`, nightly): **a line's many kept versions within I14** (D26, the systems audit's sixth round; I14). No format change, nothing saved differently and no version let go sooner: the close keeps what it bills.
  - **The cost.** A line keeps every version it ran on the day of a change until the close (the fourth round), and every recompute in service built its plan in effect by merging them all from the first, twice (world.ts recomputeInService's frame before the change and after), the page's map (`Fleet.setLines`) a third time on its main thread, and the close a fourth (settledOnto). `bench/versions.ts`: Inner Boston's longest corridor, Massachusetts Avenue (about 5.7 km, 12 stops), drawn at 05:00 every 5 minutes and every 20 overnight, then changed every 10 minutes until the close (113 changes, 114 versions kept) and six times the next night, through the host the game runs, by headways alone or by turns headways, the last stop off and back, and a model swap. On c3c2d9a: a change's handling at p95 306–333 ms and 651–808 ms over the first day (max 1,023 ms), planInEffect alone p95 131–392 ms, `Fleet.setLines` p95 135–405 ms (max 496 ms on the main thread), the close 137–487 ms. A CPU profile put 63.0 s of 66.0 s of recompute in planInEffect, 57.2 s in merge(), and 46.2 s of that in handoverOf, which wrote every stint's remaining day as a string at each candidate moment.
  - **The change** (`rules/transition.ts`, which the worker and the page's map share). A plan depends only on the versions up to it and the changes between them, never on later ones, so each step of the fold is kept under its parent step's id and what it merges from, in a bounded, least-recently-used memo (4,096 steps; 256 plans, two a fold, so about 64 settling lines at once before a fold starts again from the first version, as every fold did before), and a fold merges only from the last plan kept: a change in the frame's day merges once a fold, an undo or a recompute with nothing changed not at all, and the close needs only the handovers kept. handoverOf builds each stint's signature once and compares them as counted sets, the same decision as the sorted strings; a fold computes each distinct version's day plan once. This is the first state a module of `src/sim` keeps between calls: deterministic, exact (the plans returned are shared and never changed: checked in costs.ts, position.ts, fleet.ts and world.ts), and a pure cache of what the plain fold returns; each realm (the worker, the page) has its own.
  - **After**, the same bench: a change's handling p95 33–38 ms over the first day whatever the version count (p50 21–25 ms at 101–114 versions), planInEffect alone p95 2–7 ms, `Fleet.setLines` p95 6–10 ms, the close 3–7 ms. Left: a change on a new day moves every change's second by the day, so it merges every version again, as does a close that lets only a line's first versions go, and a fresh worker or page: with 115 versions, a change's handling 104–176 ms and `Fleet.setLines` 96–226 ms on the main thread (was 328–784 ms and 166–469 ms), once a line a day.   - **Unchanged:** `npm run bench jumps`' positions, `npm run bench parking`, `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3, byte-identical. The recompute bench's assignment, untouched, swung 108–300 ms on both trees under the shared load.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): a count of merges for a line changed every 10 minutes 60 times, at most 3 a change and 180 in all, fails on c3c2d9a with 3,600 (2k − 1 at change k). An equality guard over seeded chains of 150 changes of every kind (headways, the far and the first stop off and on, roadworks, model swaps, undos, a change made again with one field different, settledOnto), crossing midnight, holds every plan to a fold run from nothing, and the handover to a copy of c3c2d9a's handoverOf; dropping a field from the step's key, keying a trip suffix by the wrong index or using the last day plan for every step each fail it. The fold run from nothing is the new code's; its merges are the old code's, but for handoverOf, which the copy holds.

- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/rules/transition.test.ts`, `src/sim/settling.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/costs.test.ts`, `src/worker/i6.test.ts`; `npm run bench parking`, `npm run bench jumps`): **a line parks the buses a change leaves spare, so a line running overnight hands over** (D26, the systems audit's fifth round; I1, I6). No format change and no new saved field: the merged plan is still worked out from the versions kept.
  - **The bug.** After a headway cut on a line running overnight the merged plan gave each departure to the bus free longest, and a bus goes home only after idling about an hour, which buses rotating every 20 minutes never do: it kept every bus the line owned and never came to run as its day plan does. The handover then fell where both plans run out of departures, at the end of the plan's window two days on, and the vehicle layer and the costs, reading the day plan's middle day from there, put a bus driving home back at its first stop: 456 m on a fresh company's line every 10, 12 and 15 minutes cut to 15, 20 and 20 at 10:58 on day 1, and 1,360 m with Metro 40s and two changes on day 3. The spare was paid all the next day: day 2's wages $1,849.31 against $1,248.00 on days 3 and 4 on the same timetable. Versions kept across a close, laid again from an empty street, then missed the bus still out, so the Day forecast came up to $572 off the night's card.
  - **The rule** (`parkSpares`, after each of the merge's departures). Each bus of the day plan's that takes a departure after this one, out or still to pull out, needs a stand-in among the merged plan's buses: one free no later than it (its trip under way done, or its first departure for one still to pull out) and no earlier than the idle limit before its next departure, so it would not idle out the hour. They are matched latest departure first, each to the latest-free bus that can stand in; once every bus of the day plan's has one, the merged plan's others go home after the trip they are on, and otherwise none does (a bus then goes home only once it has waited out the idle limit at the first stop, as before). So the merged plan parks its spares as its day plan would, and hands over. The matching is the mirror of the textbook greedy for intervals and points, and the fifth round's verify found it always agreed with an exhaustive search on whether stand-ins exist. Across a chain of changes it can cost a departure: a bus parked for one version may be on its way home when the next version's first departures need it, and one no bus can reach then is not run (below); turning a bus round on its drive home would be a new behaviour, not kept for so few. One rule for every line, with service overnight or not, as `setHeadways.ts`' header promised ("a line with spare buses parks them and pays no driver for them"): a line that stops at night kept its spare out, and paid, until the evening's last departure, which 2026-10-13 below measured as the design (amended there; the fifth round's verify refuted the finding on that entry, and parking the spare turned out to be the fix for the jump). The lane's first rule parked the bus free longest, so another pulled out of the depot minutes later for the departure the parked one would have made (21 round trips in 13 of the bench's 2,500 longer headways; one in 2,235 random changes with the review's remedy); the matching wastes none. Weighed and not kept: refusing a handover where the plans place buses differently, which with no parking turned the 456 m jump into every bus gone at the window's end; and laying every kept version from the day before its oldest's last departure, which changed nothing once spares park and costs a fourth day of departures.
  - **The handover's second.** handoverOf's candidates at a drive out are its whole second: at the drive's own fractional second, the costs split that hour a float's width off the day plan's middle day, and neither part billed the drive (39 of 101 depot distances in `costs.test.ts`; on a random player, a $3.78 Day forecast mismatch on day 23).
  - **Numbers** (`npm run bench parking`: 10,000 seeded changes on synthetic lines, 2,500 each of longer and shorter headways and roadworks starting and ending, about half with service overnight). Handovers that fall back to the window's end: 0 (main: 10). After a longer headway, the handover comes at p50 50, p90 97, max 387 minutes with no service overnight and at p50 54, p90 85, max 337 with it (main: p50 87 to 103 and p90 750 to 964 minutes, 346 past the next midnight); the debug line's six changes, laid straight, 15 to 35 minutes. No departure is left unrun that main runs (those it leaves, between a change and its handover, no bus can reach in time). Buses sent home and then needed: 0. In 59 of the 2,500 longer headways the merged plan runs a bus fewer than its day plan for over an hour (the longest 322 minutes), where the day plan carries a bus that waits while one fewer runs every departure; every departure runs and the costs bill what runs. Chains of two or three headway changes, 1 s to 3 h apart on one day (2,500): no handover at the window's end (main: 4), the handover at p50 53, p90 144, max 645 minutes after the last change (main: 85, 807 and 2,688), and 47 departures in 46 chains left unrun that main ran, against 1 the other way (the bench copied into main's tree and run from the same directory).
  - **Sim numbers**, against main (b315d69, whose sim #133 left as it was), alternated on a loaded 4-CPU container: `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3 byte-identical (cash on day 40 $38,940, $40,733 and $99,344; the second line on days 33, 33 and 28: the pacing run changes no headway in service). `npm run bench jumps`: every movement case 0.0 m; the 48 headway changes' p95 28–33 → 35–41 ms (the parking itself); planInEffect for Inner Boston's ten corridors settling 5.5–5.8 ms (about 5.3 before). I14: `npm run bench jumps`, six runs of each tree back to back at a load of about 1, put the ten corridors' recompute with every line settling at p50 about 100 ms on both and p95 a median 138.6 → 121 ms, and the draw day's 48 headway changes and 48 stop edits at p95 medians 27.9 → 29.4 and 50.8 → 58.5 ms; the recompute bench's assignment, which this does not touch, swung either side of 150 ms on both trees with the load. A line changed every few minutes all day keeps a hundred versions or more, and planInEffect then costs hundreds of milliseconds on either tree, a little more on this one: an I14 risk before this change, still to be measured by a committed bench (since measured and brought within I14: 2026-10-16, the sixth round, above).
  - **A save made mid-settle** on an earlier build loads into the new rule as any sim fix does: on the day it loads, the Day forecast can differ from that night's card, by up to the spare bus's wages for the day, since the hours already banked were the old rule's, and a spare may be drawn somewhere else at the load; the next day agrees. On the preview, where a line cut on an overnight service stayed settling into the next day, such saves are common: a playtest starts from a fresh game or a settled one.
  - **Verified** before merge by three adversarial checks of the plans, the money and the cost: none found a defect the change brings beyond the chains above and the saves just named.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with the base's merge, the overnight transition case (every 10 minutes to every 15), the parked spare's day 2, three days of changes and a stop taken off and put back, and the fresh company's and the Metro 40s' handovers through the host fail; with the drive-out candidates unrounded, the costs test fails for 39 depots; with the lane's first rule, the depot round trip after a cut at 06:16:29 on a 6 km line fails. One existing test changed with the rule: a second change within an hour of the draw now also bills the parked bus's drive home in that hour.
