No format change: the worker gains `take-save` and `took-save`, page and worker only.
  - **The GPU cards' word on saves.** The no-GPU card and the lost-graphics card say "Your game is saved" only while the game saves itself. While it does not (the Saves chip's "Autosave off") or the browser keeps nothing at all (blocked site data, on Home before a company is founded too), they say "This game isn’t saving itself: export it before you reload." with Export this game: alone on the no-GPU card, before Reload on the lost card. Chosen over Reload asking first: Reload stays one press and the way on, and the card says what a reload loses and offers what keeps it. On 050dbdd the lost card's Reload lost the whole company with blocked site data, and everything since the store first refused with a full one (31,620 game seconds and a fare change on the audit's run). At 1152×720 the no-GPU card grows 452×86 → 452×116 px while the game isn't saving itself (its Export row); the lost card stays 452×96. Neither is chrome, so I13 is unchanged. Since amended (2026-10-19, the systems audit's seventh round): the map's start-failure cards and the replaced build's card offered Reload alone, which lost the whole company with blocked site data. While the game isn't saving itself they say so too, with Export this game before Reload; the replaced build's card then stacks its words over its buttons, at most a map card's 420 px wide (448×102 px at 1152×720; in one row it covered the Cash chip). A failed game's card still offers Reload alone, since Export went with its worker.
  - **Two tabs.** The page keeps what it last read or wrote under the live key and what it last knew the key to hold, from the window's `storage` event (`onOtherTabWrites`, in `src/platform`) or a read made before a write. The event for any key has Saves list its rows again, a slot's as the autosave's, so within Saves' 2 s listing a row names a save another tab has just kept there (the systems audit's seventh round). Not handled: a Save here, Load or Delete inside those 2 s acts on the new save, since the sim writes a slot without reading it first; it needs both tabs in Saves on one slot within two seconds. A job holding one of the game's own saves (the autosave, the quarter's start), or any job on the live key, reads the key first and is held back whole while the key holds anything else: nothing written or deleted, nothing refused, nothing owed. So the first tab to write after the other read keeps writing, and the other holds back, the saves its Load, Import, Restart this quarter or New game would make included; Save here and Export stay free, as the ways to keep its game. A removal counts as a change, so an old tab never writes its old format back into a key a newer build's tab emptied (D12).
  - **Said in the game, taken up on Home.** While left behind, the Saves chip reads "Autosave off" (a third cause, amending 2026-10-15 below; its name, tooltip and look unchanged), a notice says "Another tab changed the autosave" for 6 s once each time the tab is left behind (in the game only), Saves' line reads "Another tab changed the autosave. Export this game to keep it." in place of a full or blocked store's, the autosave's row drops "This game saves itself as it plays", and Saves lists again at each change, so the row names the browser's game. "Changed", not the lane's first "saved a newer game": a removal saves nothing, and a Load or a New game there is no further on. While Home stands the page reads the key as Home opens (a page back from the browser's cache was told nothing) and at each change, one take at a time, and the worker plays a save this build can play (`take-save`): paused, undo cleared, a new session, nothing written or set aside, the quarter's start the store's as after a reload; the page drops the replaced game's refused saves, day card, draft and refused Save here. Meanwhile Home says nothing of the change unless the take fails: said at once, Saves' line and the GPU cards' words came and went at each of the other tab's saves and the slots' rows jumped 44 px under the pointer (the review of #133). No take goes once Home is gone, nor once Home has asked for a game (a Load, an Import, a New game, Restart this quarter) until that ask is refused, so a game is never replaced in play or in the game it asked for: the lane's first cut sent one more after Continue, and on #133's first push 7 Loads from Home in 25 ended on the other tab's game with it saving every 0.67 s (both shown failing). A save this build can't play (another format, another city, a removal) leaves the game as it was, still not saving itself, and a reload then does what D12 does. A save held at the start (`unbacked`) stays held through a take, since its only copy may be the worker's, for Export.
  - **Not handled.** Chromium updates other tabs' view of `localStorage` asynchronously, so two writes landing within that lag (milliseconds) can still cross. The model picked for new lines is not reset by a take (Home has every tool down). Back to Home in a tab left behind takes the other tab's game at once, dropping the play this tab has had since, with no question: Saves' line above the button names Export, the way to keep it; asking first, or not taking a game that has moved on since it was left behind, is a later change. Since amended (2026-10-16, the sixth round, above): it asks first where there is play to lose.
  - **The lost card in reach.** High on the board the lost card sat under the cards that wait for an answer, at 60 px: on 050dbdd at 1152×720 the lender's card, which has no close, hid its words and Reload (`elementFromPoint` at 5%, 50% and 95% of their middle rows: words none of three, Reload one) and a day card its words. While a day card or the run's end is up, the GPU ladder's cards now drop to the board's foot, 80 px above the window's bottom (`.app:has(.day-card)`), which such a card leaves clear: at most 52vh below its 60 px, its foot at 458 px of 720 where the lost card's top is at 544 (592 of 768, 724 of 900). They stay on the map's layer, under the cards and panels (amending the fifth round's first lane, below). The lane's first fix lifted the lost card over the cards (101), where it hid the shortest day card's Continue (a company with nothing built: Continue at 154–181 px under the card's 72–168, at all three sizes) and Finance's tabs (3 of 4 at 1152×720, 2 of 4 at 1366×768 and 1440×900). Now, at all three sizes, the card's words and Reload and that Continue are on top at both ends and the middle, no panel button and no speed option is hidden, and the dock is 22 px below the card at 720 (`e2e/reach.spec.ts`; the lender's Restart this quarter in `e2e/gpu.spec.ts`). Since amended (2026-10-19, the seventh round): the map's start-failure cards, which stay up while the game plays on without a map, drop too. The day card and then the lender's card hid their words and Reload as they had the lost card's (`e2e/errors.spec.ts`). Since amended (2026-10-21, #143): the lender's card is on the panels' layer, in a free place from 56 px down and no taller than its place allows, and the map's cards still drop for it.
  - **I13** at 1152×720 on a fresh company (the lane's probe, on its first words): at rest 4.32% saving itself, 4.43% left behind ("Autosave off", 33 px wider); Saves 18.79% → 20.60% left behind (+44 px for the line). Its words now, measured in Saves' place for that line, take the same 292×38 px, two rows, as the first words and a blocked store's line, whose place they take, so Saves is never taller than with that line (19.13% with a blocked store's shorter autosave row).
  - **The PR checks.** Five e2e tests more, about 83 s summed on a loaded 4-CPU container, the two-tab test about 50 s of it.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/ui/format.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Contracts.test.ts`, `src/sim/demand/assign.test.ts`): **a HEADWAY figure that agrees with its verdict, and twin lines that split their changers** (D26, the systems audit's fifth round). No format change.
  - **The figure.** A HEADWAY contract's figure rounds toward its verdict (`contractMet`, the one predicate the card's colour, the day's grading and the map read): short of the ask, up to the next whole second past the float noise `contractMet` allows (`HEADWAY_HAIR_S`, 1e-6 s); met, to the nearest second. Within a minute of the ask, or where one decimal would read as a whole minute, it prints minutes and seconds ("every 10 min 1 s"); otherwise as before ("every 8.6 min"). Asks are whole seconds (`config/contracts.ts`). On f86993b two Copley lines in snow combined to 600.2499 s and printed "every 10 min" in red beside the ask "Every 10 min", and a line a second short of 20 minutes printed "every 20.0 min".
  - **Twin lines.** An exact tie (`===`) between ways off one first line (a change onto either of twin lines, or a change and a one-seat ride) splits that line's riders evenly among them, in the fast loop and the reference alike; the second leg still costs the second line's own wait (2026-10-13, "one change of line"), so twins carry no more changers than one line would. On the fixture, with Copley–Longwood–Kenmore and Kenmore–Symphony twice, the twins read 67.30 and 71.86 riders a day (0 and 4.56 changes onto them) by the order they were drawn, and the reference gave the changers to the other twin; now 69.58 and 69.58 (2.28 each) in either order, the reference 69.99 each. The express line, trips and paid riders are unchanged, and Inner Boston's assignment is identical to the rider (it has no exact tie). Not handled: ways that differ by float noise alone (by reading, two such lines drawn in opposite directions may) still go to the quicker by an ulp, which is no choice by draw order (EC-02).
  - **Sim numbers**, against f86993b: `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3 are byte-identical. I14: on a loaded 4-CPU container the recompute bench's p95 swung either side of 150 ms before and after (Inner Boston's assignment 137.7–227.8 ms before, 164.4–285.0 after); an alternating A/B in one process, 80 runs each, gave p50 102.0 → 101.2 and 95.9 → 100.9 ms, p95 154.7 → 156.5 and 153.9 → 166.7 ms. The nightly, on GitHub's runner, has the say.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/ui/{focusSource,onePress}.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Panels.test.ts`, `e2e/{menus,panels,presses,saves}.spec.ts`): **keys and focus** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round). No format change.
  - **A held key acts once on every control.** A repeat of Enter or Space on a button, a link, a dropdown's option or a menu's item, or on a dropdown's open list, is stopped at the window in the capture phase with its default (`onePress`), as a dropped click is. Other keys' repeats go on (an arrow moves, a text field types); the page's own Space and 1 to 5 drop their repeats themselves (App.tsx). The demand menu's and the route editor's own guards went. On abb5f2f a held Enter pressed a focused button at each repeat: two stop upgrades (a Shelter, then a Station), three undos, ten exports; Play flipped time, and the demand menu turned Homes on and off at each repeat of Enter.
  - **Escape and a menu's button.** Escape and a modifier alone move no focus of the player's: the focus handed back on one comes with the source the element had when the focus last left it (`focusSource`), and a press records the control it lands on as the pointer's, for the browsers that focus no button a press lands on (Safari, and Firefox on macOS; by reading: the e2e tests run in Chromium alone). So a menu the mouse opened hands its button back to the mouse as Escape closes it, whatever arrows, Home, End or Tab moved through it, and Space plays and pauses; one reached with Tab or opened from the keyboard hands it back to the keyboard, and Space opens it again. An element never focused before takes the last key's or press's source. On abb5f2f Space after Escape reopened a menu the mouse had opened.
  - **Saves' questions.** A question opened from the keyboard (a click with `detail` 0: Delete, Save here over a save, Load, Import a save file) takes the focus to its keeping answer, Keep it or Keep playing, never the one that deletes or replaces; answered from the keyboard, the focus goes back to the button that opened it, or to the slot's row once Delete emptied it, or to the panel once a set-aside save's row went with it. A mouse press moves no focus. On abb5f2f the focus fell to the page, so the next Space ran time behind the question. Not handled: Load or Import answered from Home's Saves, where the loaded game's Saves takes the place of Home's and the focus falls to the page, so Space plays the loaded game, as it does on the page; and a row or the panel holding the focus leaves Space to the page too.
  - **Panels past the edge.** The panel layer clips what hangs past the window (`overflow: clip`), so the page never scrolls. A control the keyboard focuses (`:focus-visible`) past either edge brings its panel across far enough to show it 12 px inside the window, and the panel stays there, its dragged place with it (`showing`). The pointer's focus moves no panel, nor does a dropdown's open list, portaled to the page with its place fixed as it opened. On abb5f2f a Tab into a panel dragged past the right edge scrolled the whole game 272 px left, and panels then opened and dragged 272 px from where they should.
  - **Create's flag.** A load from a slot, a file or Restart this quarter clears App's Create-in-flight flag (`creating`), as the created line does; nothing else clears it. A new game needs no clear: it has no line, and its load carries no date. On abb5f2f a Create refused, or answered together with its undo, left the flag set, and a game loaded later opened Lines on its newest line.
  - **The PR checks.** Five e2e tests more (104 on the fixture) move Playwright's shard boundaries, insert's and line's tests to the first third: about 62–66 s more summed, timed on a loaded 4-CPU container, where CI runs 2–3 times faster.
- **2026-10-16** (`e2e/errors.spec.ts`, `e2e/home.spec.ts`, `e2e/start.spec.ts`): **a map that cannot start said with Reload, a founding card that can be left, and loads that start it clean** (D26, the systems audit's fifth round). No format change.
  - **The map's start failures.** Each status the map has words for ('no-city-file', 'empty', 'failed', 'no-gpu', 'lost') is now one card high on the board (top 72 px, as the GPU ladder's were), every one but 'no-gpu' with Reload through `src/platform`. 'failed' covers the map's file unread after a server error or a dropped connection, MapLibre failing to mount and the 60 s load timeout, and now reads "Boston’s map couldn’t be loaded." (was "The map could not start on this computer."). On abb5f2f its words sat centred under Home's card and then under the founding card, with no Reload: `elementFromPoint` at the notice's ends and middle found Home, then the founding card, all three times. The cards stay on the map's layer, under the cards and panels: at 1152×720 their place alone clears Home and the founding card, while on the replaced build's layer (the lane's first fix) the no-GPU card, which stays up while the game plays on, would hide the day card's first rows. While the game's own card shows (`failed-card`), the map's says nothing (MapView's `quiet`), so a dropped connection that fails both city files shows one card with one Reload. The map's cards take the game's font: MapLibre's container sets its own on what it holds. Not handled: in a background tab the load's 60 s timer can fire while MapLibre waits for frames, so the card may show for a moment when the tab comes back, until the map's first frame (`status.ts`). Since amended (2026-10-16, the fifth round's files lane, above): while a day card or the run's end is up, the GPU ladder's cards drop to the board's foot, on the map's layer still.
  - **Back to Home on the founding card**, under Found the company, at Home's and Saves' weight: the tool goes down, every panel closes and Home shows, with no pause (the card's game has not started; a pause would write an autosave of the unnamed game and a session command). Home then shows New game and Saves, with no Continue. Chosen over the Saves chip on the card, which would bring "Autosave off" onto it and break the held save's rule that its notice waits on the founding card, "where no button opens Saves". Escape on the card still does nothing. I13: the founding card at 1152×720, 13.4% → 14.9% (340×271 → 340×309 px).
  - **A load starts the card clean.** The founding card is keyed by the load (`sim.loaded.at`), so a slot, a file or Restart this quarter holding an unnamed game opens it empty, on Red, with Found the company disabled. On abb5f2f it kept the name and colour typed for the company it replaced, and one press founded the loaded game as "Back Bay Transit" in Blue.
- **2026-10-16** (`e2e/i13.spec.ts`, `e2e/time.spec.ts`, `e2e/errors.spec.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Contracts.test.ts`): **the chip row late in a game, held speed keys, and a stopped game's map** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round). No format change.
  - **The row's last chip.** The checklist's step, or the Contracts chip in its place, gives up room as the Undo chip does (at least 6em, at most its whole label), its medals kept whole and its whole words in its title and accessible name. Its grow, a thousand times the Undo's, takes the free room first, so the Undo is down to its 6em (84 px) before the last chip is cut at all, and the row wraps only when 6em of each does not fit. I13 at 1152×720 in the countdown late in a game (Year 10, paused at 5,400×, a fare's undo, three medals and "3 on offer", or "4/5 Close a day in profit"), abb5f2f → after: with "Autosave off", 10.08% (two rows, 1003×62 px) → 5.97% (one row; with Inner Boston's numbers the last chip is cut to 117 px, "Contracts…" and its three medals); without it, with Inner Boston's numbers, 10.22% (1036×62) → 5.97%; with the fixture's numbers and no "Autosave off" the Contracts chip stays whole (159 px) beside a 110 px Undo. Every other I13 number is unchanged; "Contracts 1 held" is now one span, 0.6 px narrower, so the earlier countdown cases' Undo is 1 px wider at the same 5.97%. The three new cases add about 40 s to the PR checks.
  - **A held speed key.** A held 1 to 5 picks its speed once: its repeats send nothing, as Space's do. A speed command for the speed already running changes nothing in the worker: no clock reset, no autosave, no snapshot and no entry in the Export session's commands, the speed menu picking the running speed included; a pause sent while paused still saves, as Home's does. On abb5f2f, holding 5 with a repeat every 33 ms for 3 s ran 0 game seconds against 16,200, wrote 92 autosaves against 6 and recorded 91 session commands against 1. Older session files replay to the same hash (`replay.ts`), since a dropped re-pick never changed state.
  - **A stopped game's map.** Once the worker fails, the map's clock stops (`rate` 0), so the vehicle layer asks for no frame after one repaint, as while paused (render only on change, PLAN §6). On abb5f2f it asked for a frame after every frame until a reload (8 → 14 in 2 s, `e2e/errors.spec.ts`). In that repaint the buses go back to the last snapshot's clock, by at most the layer's 250 ms run-on: 15 game seconds at 60×, 22 game minutes at 5,400×.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/world.test.ts`, `src/sim/commands/setRoute.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/reportCard.test.ts`; `npm run bench recompute`, nightly): **I14 with depots, Edit route's depot room, and the report card's last day** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round). No format change: the drives' cache is derived and never saved, and the close's rows merge by kind as before.
  - **I14 with depots.** Every recompute routed each line's drives to and from every depot again, though a drive changes only with the line's first stop or its depot: with ten lines and five depots on Inner Boston, 166 router searches a recompute against 106 with none, `lineServices` 118 ms against 6, and a building command 230–350 ms at p50 (set a stop's tier 238 ms, take a stop off a line 357 ms, an undo 258 ms; the audit's harness). The world now keeps each drive by its depot's node and first stop's node (`depotDrive`, a cache on the world's router, each recompute keeping what it used), and the worker's line views read the same drives. The router depends only on the graph, so no number moves (the pacing runs are identical byte for byte). `bench/recompute.ts` adds the whole recompute with ten lines of three Sparrows and five depots: p50 196.0 / p95 226.1 ms → p50 90–100 / p95 135–154 ms over three runs on a shared container, where the plain assignment's p95 swung 121–405 ms meanwhile; the nightly times it on GitHub's runner. A command that moves a first stop or adds a depot still routes its new drives once.
  - **Edit route's depot room.** With every depot full, a route needing two more buses said "2 more Sparrows" and "Depots have room for 2 more": the line rule ran with the line's own buses taken off the company's, so the room counted them as free. Route, extend and insert verdicts now name the depots' real room (`editRefusal`, one predicate for the card and the command): "Your depots are full", as the headway card says; with room for 2, room 2 where it read 4. Whether a change is refused is unchanged. Not changed: the model swap's card names its room with the line's own buses in it, since a swap sells them.
  - **The report card's last day.** The close graded the quarter before posting the day's interest, so each quarter's last day of interest was graded in no quarter (the fixture's Money 73.5016 where the quarter's rows give 73.3700). The close now posts the day's contract rewards, then its interest, then grades the card and posts its grant or fine, then awards medals; interest is charged on what is owed before the grant or fine. The pacing runs (40 days, seeds 1–3) are unchanged, as no quarter owes there; run owing from the start (`PACING_RESERVE_CENTS=-15000000`), each card's Money grade falls by 0.03–0.09 and its payout by $0.01–$29.78 (seed 1, day 8: 69.8080 → 69.7534, $13,171.05 → $13,153.59), seed 3's day-24 card prints 67.8 where it printed 67.9, cash on day 40 moves by $62–$85, and every milestone day and minute is the same.
- **2026-10-16** (`src/sim/settling.test.ts`; `npm run bench jumps`, `npm run bench pacing`, `npm run bench firstline`): **a second change in a day, after the first has handed over** (D26, the systems audit's fourth round; the M4 spec, item 4; I1, I6). No format change: `EarlierRun` is unchanged, only how long the versions are kept.
  - **The bug.** On any day but a line's draw day, a change made after the line had handed over to its own day plan let go of its kept versions, so a second change that day dropped the version that ran before the first: the merged plan then held the first change's version as if it had run since midnight. Finance's Day forecast (the Breakdown's and Lines' running, wages and deadhead, and Costs/day) re-costed the hours before the first change at its timetable, and when both changes fell in one clock hour that hour banked off the rebuilt plan. On the fixture, the debug line every 20 minutes from 05:00 on day 1, every 10 from 08:00 on day 2 (handing over at 08:20) and every 15 from 08:40: forecast $485.97 running, $1,456.63 wages and $20.64 to and from the depot, against $433.59, $1,379.14 and $13.76 banked; hour 8 banked $32.01, $78.00 and $0 (a leg no bus ran, and no drive out for the bus that pulled out at 08:xx). In the audit's seeded runs of changes over three days, a day missed by as much as $2,161 of running forecast against $750 banked.
  - **The rule.** A line changed in service keeps every version it ran on the day of the change until that day's close; it lets them go at a change only if it had settled onto its own day plan by that day's midnight. At the close (`settle`), a line past its handover lets every version go, as before; one still settling (a change late in the evening hands over the next morning, about 04:56 on the fixture after 23:30) keeps only the versions from the last it had settled onto before it changed again (`settledOnto`, one pass of the merges `planInEffect` makes), so its plan from midnight is the same, minute by minute and hour by hour, and a run of late changes carries one or two versions past midnight, not a day's. The draw day is this rule's first case; `drawnAt` decides only that a line's fares count from the hour it was drawn.
  - **After.** The same case forecasts $430.68, $1,370.47 and $17.20 and day 2 banks exactly that; hour 8 banks $29.10, $69.33 and $3.44 (on 43554a9 the day banked $2.91 and $8.67 more and $3.44 less than its buses did). The one-change control is unchanged ($608.19, $1,381.30, $17.20). Three seeded runs of changes over two days, some after a handover and some moving the first stop, meet each day's card to the cent and bank each hour gone as forecast. A line changed every 50 minutes from 06:00 to 23:30 for five days carries 2 versions past each close (22 without the trim; on 43554a9, 1 or 2), its save 4.8 KB.
  - **Sim numbers**, against 43554a9: `npm run bench firstline` and the 40-day pacing run on seeds 1–3 are unchanged (cash on day 40 $38,940, $40,733 and $99,344; the second line on days 33, 33 and 28). I14: Massachusetts Avenue on Inner Boston changed 48 times through an ordinary day, the last at 23:50, keeps 48 versions at a p95 of 46.8 ms a change; the close's `settledOnto` over them takes 6.7 ms and the whole close 54.1 ms.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/platform/saveStore.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **a full store said as full, Save here on its own row, and "Autosave off"** (D26, the systems audit's third round; D12, DP-06). No format change: the worker's `store` message gains `slot` and says which save `owed` marks (`autosave` or `quarter`), page and worker only.
  - **Full or blocked.** `src/platform` tells the two apart by the error's name alone: a QuotaExceededError is `full`, anything else (a SecurityError from blocked site data) `blocked`. Measured in Chromium only (`e2e/saves.spec.ts`, the store filled by other keys of the origin). A full store's notice reads "No room for saves: delete a save", naming no control, since the Saves chip may read "Autosave off" by then, and Saves' line "No room for saves. Delete a save, or export this game to keep it.": Export too, since other data of the same origin can take the room with nothing in Saves to delete, and "for saves", not "this game", since the quarter's start may be refused while the autosave is kept. Blocked site data keeps its words. With both pending, Saves' line says blocked; the notice keeps the words of the page's first refusal, once a page as before.
  - **Save here.** A refused Save here owes the sim nothing and is said on its slot's row alone, "Didn’t fit: delete a save to make room" or "Not kept: this browser isn’t keeping saves", until that slot is saved, any delete in Saves is kept (it made room, so the advice is followed; a press refused again says so again) or another game loads (New game, Load, Import, Restart this quarter). On 029387a it raised the once-a-page notice and held Saves' line only until a write was kept, so at 5,400× the next autosave (every 0.67 s) took it down and a second press left the slot Empty with nothing said. A refused delete still raises the notice and holds Saves' line until a write is kept.
  - **"Autosave off".** While the game is not saving itself, the Saves chip reads "Autosave off", in the countdown's red at the chip row's 14 px (with Saves open, in the pressed chip's colours): while a save the start could not set aside is held, or while the browser has refused the autosave and kept none since (a full store until a delete makes room; blocked site data for the page's life). It keeps those words while Saves is open, so its accessible name ("Saves: autosave off", which keeps "Saves") does not change and it does not move under the pointer as Saves opens; its tooltip reads "This game isn’t saving itself: open Saves to see why". On Home the held save's notice "Not autosaving: see Saves" stays until Saves opens, as before; in the game the chip says it for as long as it lasts, where the notice went for good once Saves had opened. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above): also while another tab has changed the autosave since this tab last read or wrote it.
  - **I13 at 1152×720.** The chip is 105 px against "Saves"' 72, and the top bar's right edge moves from 104 px to 137 px, so a full row's Undo gives up the 33 px (165 → 132 px with the fixture's numbers, 131 → 98 px with Inner Boston's). In the countdown with the save held: 5.98% with both sets of numbers, where the notice beside the full row would have taken 6.61% (5.98% plus its 169×31 px, 0.63%, added from two measurements: 029387a cannot reach the countdown without opening Saves, which took the notice down). With the save held: at rest 4.95% → 4.4%, with a line 5.81% → 5.3%, the stop taken off ("Undo: Take Stop 2 off Line 1") 5.96% → 5.4%, and so with Inner Boston's numbers 6.07% → 5.5%; Saves 24.5% → 24.6% (the chip's longer words), with Load's or Import's question 24.6%; Home 8.7%, unchanged. Every other I13 number is unchanged (at 1152×720: rest 4.3%, with a line 5.2%, Saves 24.3%, Finance with purchases 24.6%; the busy day card 23.5% and, at 1366×768, 19.5%).
  - **Its limit.** In the tightest case measured (the countdown with Inner Boston's numbers) the Undo is 98 px, 14 px above its 6em floor (84 px), so a row wider still (more contracts on offer, a second medal) would wrap with "Autosave off" where it would not with "Saves". Not measured; I13's countdown test fails if the row wraps. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): measured late in a game, it wrapped (10.08%), and the row's last chip now gives up room once the Undo is at its 6em.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/sim/rules/transition.test.ts`, `src/sim/settling.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/finance.test.ts`, `src/worker/{host,i6}.test.ts`, `e2e/line.spec.ts`; `npm run bench jumps`, now nightly): **buses after a line changes, and a line's first day** (D26, the systems audit's third round; the M4 spec, item 4; I6). No format change: a version that ran no bus is an ordinary kept version, its plan's buses 0.
  - **A second change.** Each kept version carries whether every change since kept its model (`sameModel`) and its first stop too (`carriesOn`). A bus finishing a trip of an older version goes home if any change since moved the first stop or swapped the model, and a sold bus is never counted as the line's. On 5f4bce3 a second change within a round trip of one that moved the first stop or swapped the model brought the old buses back: jumps of 1.0–2.8 km in the audit, 2,524 m and 2,137 m in `i6.test.ts`'s seeded changes in a row, and a sold Sparrow running Metro 40 trips. Now no bus moves over 5 m.
  - **Service with no bus out.** Every change in service is merged, with buses out or not, and a line just drawn changes from a version that ran no bus, so its buses pull out of the depot for the departures they can reach, as each morning's do, and their drives bill under To and from the depot. Before, a line drawn at 10:20 had three buses on its streets at once and no drive out billed; overnight service turned on with every bus home did the same. The close now retimes for an event before it lets settled lines go (`step.ts`), so roadworks or snow at midnight after a change that day leave every bus where it was.
  - **The draw day.** A line keeps the version before its draw, and every version since, until that day's close (`drawnAt`, the one predicate for its costs and Finance's forecast), and past a handover the merged plan carries on from what ran instead of starting again from the latest version's day plan. So a later change that day still finds nothing run before the draw. Finance's Day forecast counts the line's fares and subsidy from the clock hour it was drawn (the day banks riders by the whole hour) and its Bus fuel and maintenance, Driver wages and To and from the depot from its first drive out, as the day banks them; Costs/day reads the same column. Depot and stop upkeep in the column stay a whole day's. On the fixture (against the lane's own first fix, which let a drawn line's versions go once it had handed over), the debug line drawn at 15:20 and changed at 18:00: $3,110.80 of fares and subsidy and $2,073.82 of costs forecast (a whole day) → $1,374.45 and $877.99, the costs exactly what day 1 banks. Drawn at 15:01 and changed at 15:31 after a handover at 15:30, hour 15 billed $32.01 running, $78.00 wages and $0 of depot drives (the 15:00 departure no bus ran) → $23.28, $52.00 and $10.32. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): every day, not the draw day alone, a line keeps every version it ran until that day's close, and `drawnAt` decides the fares only.
  - **A new game's first line**, drawn at 05:00, skips the departures no bus can reach from the depot in time, its 05:00 departure among them, and day 1's line costs meet day 1's forecast to the cent (`host.test.ts`). Day 1's depot and stop upkeep are still 19 hours of 24 against a whole day's forecast.
  - **Sim numbers**, against cf80298 (e3f9bb9 changes no sim file; 029387a's fold leaves every figure the same to the cent). `npm run bench firstline` is unchanged: Washington Street, $2,523 on day 2, 18 of 18 fit, the four-bus line 1.1506×. The pacing run over 40 days, seeds 1–3, holds every band and PLAN §3 target (founding day 2 $1,109; the second line on days 33, 33 and 28; the first card at 64.8, 65.8 and 64.8 minutes). What moves: the second line's gain on its draw day, $995 → $1,002, $995 → $1,002 and $906 → $912, and cash on day 40, $38,896 → $38,940, $40,691 → $40,733 and $99,300 → $99,344, all from the draw-day billing.
  - **Cost of it (I14).** A line changed every 20 minutes through its draw day keeps every version, and the worker routed each kept version's streets for the vehicle layer afresh at every refresh. On Inner Boston, with Massachusetts Avenue drawn and then its headways changed 48 times, a change took 27 ms at the first and 262 ms at the 48th (p95 284 ms); with a stop added before its first and taken off again in turn, 38 ms and 306 ms (p95 290 ms). The worker now keeps each version's routed streets, by its stops and depot, from one refresh to the next while a line still keeps a version on them: p95 31 ms and 57 ms, the slowest of the 48 changes 34 ms and 65 ms. Carried only within one refresh, the route edits' p95 was 166 ms. Measured with `npm run bench jumps` as committed, with the lane's diff without the cache, and with the cache, alone on the machine. `bench/jumps.ts` fails when either p95 is over 150 ms, when a change is refused or when the versions are not kept, and now runs nightly. Not in the bench: the page prepares every kept version again on each change of the lines (`Fleet.setLines`), 2.7 ms at 2 versions and 7.5 ms at 49 in vitest; each frame's cost stays flat (since measured, by `bench/versions.ts`, and kept from one change to the next: 2026-10-16, the sixth round, above). Since the fourth round (2026-10-16, above) a line changed on any day keeps its versions so: the same bench holds for it.
  - **An older build.** By reading: a save written on a line's draw day after it has handed over holds versions an older build's merge drops, so an older tab that loads it reads that line's running costs as NaN until the close. Web saves are not kept across builds as a promise (D12), so no format bump.

At 1366×768 and 1440×900 the undo shows whole (4.8%, 3.9%). Since amended (2026-10-15, above): in the game the Saves chip says "Autosave off" in place of that notice, 5.98% in the countdown with the save held.

- **2026-10-15** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/Notices.test.ts`, `e2e/errors.spec.ts`): **saves the game owes, and a stopped game that shows nothing of itself** (D26, the systems audit's third round; D12). No format change.
  - **Owed by key.** The sim marks its own two writes (`owed`): the autosave and the quarter's start. When the browser refuses one, the page keeps its key and tells the sim (`store-refused`), and the sim owes that save; after any delete in Saves (a slot, a set-aside save, the save the start could not set aside) it writes what it owes at once, the autosave first, then the quarter's start from the copy it holds in memory, before the slots are listed again. A refused owed write is owed again; nothing retries on its own. While the start's hold lasts, the autosave stays owed and nothing is written over the held save.
  - **Saves' line.** "This browser isn’t keeping saves" holds while a refused key of the game's own is not kept since (a kept write of that key, or a delete of it, ends it), so a kept autosave no longer hides a quarter's start the browser refused, and a delete's job (removes only) never takes it down. Any other refusal (a Save here, a delete, a read) holds it only until a write is kept, as before (since amended, 2026-10-15, above: a full store's line names no room, Delete and Export, and a Save here refused is said on its slot's row alone). The refused keys live in the page alone: a reload forgets them while the save is still not kept. The notice still says it once a page. Before, any kept job took the line down, a delete's too, though nothing had been saved, and a reload then lost the play since (the audit's case: a depot and its $150,000).
  - **A stopped game.** Once the worker fails, the game renders no panel (Finance, Lines, Contracts, Saves, Saves opened from Home included, a stop's card, the new-line card, the route editor), no card beside the pointer, no day card, no lender's card, no depot tint and no line being drawn: only the card with Reload. The map, its credit and the compass stay. Before, they stayed on screen with buttons that sent to a worker that was gone.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/ui/focusSource.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/{RouteEditor,Tools,useSim}.test.ts`, `src/worker/host.test.ts`, `e2e/route.spec.ts`, `e2e/time.spec.ts`, `e2e/undo.spec.ts`): **keys that act once, and undos answered** (D26, the systems audit's third round). No format change.
  - **A mouse press takes Space back.** A pointer press clears where the focus came from (`focusSource`), so Space after a click on a button that Tab had once reached pauses and plays instead of pressing the button again (a second purchase undone from the Undo chip, Finance closed). Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): Escape moves no focus of the player's either, so a menu's button gets back the source it had when the menu opened.
  - **A held key acts once.** Space's repeats send nothing (no speed command, no autosave), as the demand menu and the tilt keys already did; the route editor's arrows drop a held Enter's repeats. Holding 1 to 5 still re-picks the same speed, which changes nothing on screen. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): each re-pick stalled the clock and saved, so a held 1 to 5 now picks its speed once, and the worker ignores a pick of the speed already running. And since (2026-10-16, above): a held Enter or Space presses every control and dropdown list once (`onePress`), and the demand menu's and the route editor's own guards went.
  - **The route editor keeps its rows.** Each row is keyed by its stop (or a new stop's click) and how many identical rows come before it, not by its place, so a stop moved from the keyboard keeps its arrow focused, or its other arrow at the list's end, and a second Space or Enter moves it again. × still drops the focus with its row.
  - **Undos answered.** The worker answers every undo once: `undone` when it took a command back, `refused` otherwise (`not_started` included). The page counts the undos it sent and the newest the sim did (`Sim.undos`, `{sent, done}`); the answer is page-and-worker only, in neither the save nor the session file. Save route's guard and Create's (`createWaits`) let go only on an undo sent after the press that the sim did; one only sent, or refused while time runs ("Time has passed since then"), lets nothing go. So Save route and an Undo answered in one render leave the editor open on the player's edit with Save route enabled; answered apart, the editor closes when the save lands, as before.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/useSim.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **saves that keep what they cannot set aside** (D26, the systems audit's second round; D12 and DP-06). No format change.
  - **The hold.** The start's set-aside is read back once. If the backup did not land (no room), the live key keeps the old save's only copy and the game writes nothing there: its autosaves are owed, the quarter's save and the slots are kept as usual. Only Delete in Saves (after a question) or a later page load ends the hold; nothing retries the backup. The notice "Not autosaving: see Saves" is untimed, shown only where a button opens Saves, and gone once Saves opens or the hold ends (since amended, 2026-10-15, the third audit round, above: said on Home alone, the Saves chip saying "Autosave off" in the game while the hold lasts); the autosave's row names the old save, "not set aside · this game isn’t saving itself", with Export (`interbus-<city>-not-set-aside-<why>.json`) and Delete. One failure, one notice: the set-aside's refusal never says "This browser isn’t keeping saves". Before, founding wrote over the only copy.
  - **Restart this quarter** reopens the quarter's save as this page last wrote it, kept in memory, so it works for the page's life where the browser keeps no site data; after a reload it is read from the store, as before.
  - **Questions.** Once a company is founded, Load and Import a save file ask "Replace this game / Keep playing", in the game and on Home; before, there is no game to lose and both go straight ahead. One question is open at a time, and while a row's question is open, its other buttons hide (Export this game too, beside Import's question).
  - **A slot from another bake** is loadable only if its stops find this bake's streets, the check Load makes (`worldFrom`); otherwise it is listed as "A save with stops off the new map", with Export and Delete and no Load. Each answer is cached by the save's text; the first listing after a rebake builds up to three worlds once.

  - **Layers.** One set of tokens in `app.css`: the panels (10), the menus and the cards beside the pointer (20), the cards that wait for an answer (the founding card, the day's close and the run's end, 100) and the replaced build's card (150). The panels draw inside one layer of their own, so raising one never lifts it over a menu, however many are raised. The dock is in the menus' layer (its transform holds the Build menu), so a dragged panel stops with its heading above it (`inReach`: the window's height less the dock's 72 px and 48 px of panel) (since the third audit round, below: 160 px of panel, so it ends above the dock); the map's credit and the build chip sit just above the menus. A dropdown's list, at 200 before, shares the menus' layer: over every panel, under the day card. Since amended (2026-10-21, the ninth audit round's fixes, #143): the run's end has its card on the panels' layer, in a free place beside them, and no panel opened after it covers it, as it stays until Restart this quarter; at the top centre among the cards it covered a panel opened third.
  - **A press away.** A press that closes a menu does nothing else only when it lands on the map's canvas (`onCanvas`, shared with `onePress`); a press on a control in the map, the compass or the lost-graphics card's Reload, closes the menu and works the control, as a press on any other control does.
  - **Keys.** Tab and Shift+Tab with a dropdown's list open close it and go on from its button, as the browser's own select; the Build menu comes after its button in the tab order, closes when the focus leaves it, and Escape on a tool gives the focus back to Build.
  - **The held double-click** on the map tilts only when the map heard the click before it (`heardLast`), so a double-click whose first click was a Home button's no longer flips the board.
- **2026-10-15** (`src/ui/onePress.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/Tools.test.ts`, `e2e/presses.spec.ts`): **one press, one command** (D26, the systems audit's second round).
  - **The rule.** One window listener in the capture phase (`onePress`, installed once by App, so Home's buttons too): a person's click with `detail` 1 on a button, a link, an option or a menu item, anywhere but the map's canvas (the compass and the lost-graphics card's Reload count), arms it; every later click of that run (`detail` above 1) and its `dblclick` is dropped wherever it lands, before React or MapLibre hears it; the next click with `detail` 0 or 1 ends it. Clicks the page makes itself (Export's download, Import's file picker) are never dropped and never end it. No control asks for a double-click; one that starts on the map or on plain text is the map's, as before. Since amended (the third audit round, below): a run that starts on the map keeps its later clicks only while they land on the map; once one lands off it (on a card that opened under the pointer), the rest of the run is dropped.
  - **A held key** is one press too, since the fourth audit round (2026-10-16, above): a repeat of Enter or Space on a control or a dropdown's list is dropped by the same listener.
  - **What it costs.** The browser counts clicks at one spot within its double-click interval as one run, so quick repeats on one button count once: the Undo chip takes back one thing a run (Ctrl+Z still repeats), and the route editor's × drops one stop a run. Chosen over per-button guards, which round one tried and which missed Close, the Undo chip and Export.
  - **Create** also waits for its own answer, as Save route does: shut from the press until a line newer than the press stands, a Create is refused, an undo is sent, the tool is put down or another game loads (`createWaits`), so a second press before a slow city's answer buys nothing; a refresh of the lines that is not its answer (a day's close, roadworks) leaves it shut. Since amended (2026-10-15, the third audit round, below): the undo must be one sent after the press that the sim did (`undone`); one only sent, or refused while time runs, leaves Create shut.

  - **A mode change cut short.** Once the camera rests after any move, a 3D board below 25° eases to the pitch its change was aiming for, and a 2D board not flat or not facing north eases flat and north (`resettle`, tilt.ts), checked one frame after the move ends, so a move that cut another short counts as moving. A 3D board stopped between 25° and 60° stays where it is.
  - **Loaded games.** The depots' layer is keyed by each depot's id and place, and the city event's by its kind, days and place (its label and its stretch's ends), since ids restart and every game starts from one seed; the map's `data-depots` says where the depots were last drawn.
  - **Lost graphics.** From MapLibre's `webglcontextlost` until the style it rebuilds has loaded, the game's layers wait as before the first load, so T, Homes and Jobs, a tool or Place a depot reach no missing style. MapLibre 6.11.2 (`src/ui/map.ts`, `_contextRestored`) sets the kept style before it fires `webglcontextrestored`, and that style has no custom layer, so once it loads the buses' layer goes back on and the buildings are set to the mode at once. A style lost again before it loaded is not kept: the board stays blank, and the card with Reload stays.
- **2026-10-14** (`src/ui/shell/Panels.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/besidePointer.test.ts`, `e2e/panels.spec.ts`, `e2e/dropdown.spec.ts`, `e2e/sim.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **panels, dropdowns and the cards beside the pointer that work** (D26, the systems audit's panels lane; amends D25 item 6).
  - **Panels.** A panel in a free place takes a row only with room for 160 px of it above the dock (72 px from the window's bottom), and a CSS variable, `--room`, caps each panel at the room below its top, so what does not fit, or grows later (Saves before its slots arrive, the inspector switched to a longer line), scrolls inside it; a panel dragged low shrinks to that room, down to 160 px. With no row free, a panel opens at the top right as far down as a step while 160 px stay above the dock. A panel never dragged stays whole when the window narrows; one dragged keeps its heading's first 48 px in reach. Since amended (the third audit round, below): every panel's top stays 160 px above the dock, dragged or placed, and 48 px of its width on screen sideways. A panel opened, or shown again while open (the Contracts chip, Take a contract, the line card's Finance link, a line or stop clicked), comes to the front. Left as it is: panels clamped one by one on a narrower window can come to overlap.
  - **Dropdowns.** The list is measured unseen, then opens below its button, or above where there is more room above, its height capped to the room on its side; it opens at the current choice, and the arrow keys, Home and End keep the highlighted choice in view. It is not limited sideways: a list wider than its button near the right edge can still run past it.
  - **Cards beside the pointer.** The dot card and the cards of Place a stop and Place a depot share one placement (`besidePointer`), flipping to the pointer's other side at the window's edge. A camera move under a still pointer reports the point now under it, so a dot's card goes once its dot moves away and the site cards judge the place a click would use (GD-05).
  - **I13** is unchanged: at 1152×720 at rest 4.3%, Saves at its tallest 24.3% and Finance with a bus bought 24.6%, as before (budget 26%).
- **2026-10-14** (`src/worker/host.test.ts`, `src/sim/save/save.test.ts`, `src/ui/shell/{Saves,Notices}.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`, `e2e/saves.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`): **saves that work** (D26, the systems audit's saves lane). No format change: `SAVE_FORMAT`, the schema and `schema.lock.json` are as they were.
  - **Rows.** A slot this game cannot load says why, as the set-aside rows do ("Slot 1 · A save from an older build (format 14)", or from a newer build, another city or one it can't read), never loads, and has Export (`export-slot`, the slot's own text as `interbus-boston-slot-N.json`) and Delete. Save here over a slot that holds anything, readable or not, asks first ("Replace Slot N" or "Keep it"), with Export still beside it, so a save this game can't load can be kept before it goes. Such a slot is not moved to a backup key on its own, since that key could hold the autosave's own set-aside save of the same format; the player's Export is how it is kept. The autosave has a row of its own, first, as a slot's (day, company, cash, lines, buses, saved when), in place of the hint "This game saves itself as it plays", now the row's tooltip. Saves lists its saves again as the store keeps writes, at most every 2 s (an autosave every 0.67 s at 5,400×), since each listing has the worker read every save.
  - **I13.** The autosave's row makes Saves at its tallest (every slot full and a set-aside save listed) **24.3%** of 1152×720, from 22.3% (budget 26%; 19.2% at 1366×768 and 15.5% at 1440×900).
  - **A browser that keeps no saves.** When the store refuses a write or a read (blocked site data, a full disk), a notice says "This browser isn’t keeping saves" once, timed only while it shows, and Saves says so with Export, until a write is kept again. Before, each failure went only to the console and a reload lost the game. Since amended (2026-10-15, above): a backup the start cannot write is not this failure; it holds the live key and says "Not autosaving: see Saves" instead. Amended again in the third audit round (2026-10-15, above): Saves' line holds while a save of the game's own the browser refused (the autosave, the quarter's start) is not kept since, and a delete in Saves writes what the game owes at once; any other refusal holds it until a write is kept, as before. And a third time (2026-10-15, above): a full store (a QuotaExceededError) says "No room for saves: delete a save", Saves' line names Delete and Export, and these words stay blocked site data's.
  - **Set aside off the map.** A save of this city from another bake whose stops find no street is set aside under `interbus.backup.<city>.off-street`, its own key, and listed as "A save with stops off the new map"; before, it shared `other-city` with a save from another city, so either could replace the other. A save set aside that way by an earlier build is still under `other-city` and still reads as another city's.
  - **Session files.** The save reader reads a file whose `kind` is `interbus-session` (Export session) as unreadable, so importing one is refused as a save it can't read, not as one from an older build.
  - **Restart this quarter** (D10) is refused ("No save from this quarter's start") when the quarter save's company differs in name or colour from the game being played, as after loading another company's ended game; it used to bring back the company played before. A save carries no game id (adding one is a format change), so two games of the same name and colour still pass; the failure card's Restart is then refused with that notice.
- **2026-10-14** (`src/sim/pacing.test.ts`, `src/sim/economy/startingCash.test.ts`; `npm run bench pacing` with `PACING_SEED` and `npm run bench firstline`, both nightly; `npm run bench report`): **the tuning freeze's starting cash and speed: k 1.333, and the ladder's 4 at 2,700×** (the M5 spec, item 2; PLAN §3; D7; I10; amends D25's ladder), measured after the report card's Coverage fix (below). Six variants were measured on the fixture and Inner Boston, each from 77d90ca, then judged and the winner re-measured over twelve seeds.
  - **What changed.** k goes from 1.2 to **1.333**, so a new company starts with **$474,548** (was $427,200) and keeps $118,548 after the founding recipe (was $71,200). The speed ladder's 4 goes from 1,800× (half an hour a second) to **2,700×** (45 minutes a second), a game day in 32 s instead of 48; 1×, 60×, 600× and 5,400× stay. Neither moves a save: the starting cash is written at founding (D7), the speed is not in the save, and the schema hash is unchanged. The grant's bar, the medals, the founding headways and the contract asks are unchanged.
  - **Why both.** k brings the second line earlier in game days; the speed turns game days into fewer minutes (the rate enters only the minutes, `src/sim/pacing.ts`, so game days do not depend on it). On Inner Boston, seeds 1, 2 and 3: the second line on **day 33 (23.4 min), day 33 (23.4) and day 28 (20.8)**, against PLAN §3's 15–25; at k 1.2 and 1,800× they came at 44.5, 42.1 and 27.7 min, and k alone at 31.7, 31.7 and 27.7. Profit on day 1 at 5.4 min and the report card and bronze on day 8 at **9.1 min** (11.0 before) on every seed; silver on day 29 (20.3 min), 28 (19.8) and 28 (21.3); no gold. At 2,700× the on-target days are 18–35, so seeds 1 and 2 have two days of margin. Seed 1 over 200 days: seven lines and three depots, 4,384 trips a day, cash $1,169,949 (five lines, 3,621 and $1,865,169 at k 1.2), cards 63.6–68.7, Coverage the lowest at 8.9–31.6.
  - **Game days are still long, and speed does not change that.** The spec expected 6–10 game days to a second line (V1: about 15); Inner Boston takes 28–33. The founding line nets $1,109 on day 2 at its best fare, a report card pays about $13k a quarter, and on seeds 1–3 no contract the founding line can meet pays before day 27: it reaches two of Inner Boston's six landmarks (two of the fixture's three). The fixture's second line comes sooner for the same reason. This is for P1 and P2 to feel; P2 should note which speeds testers use, since a player at 5,400× reaches day 33 at about 15 minutes.
  - **Across seeds it is not one number.** The scripted player draws its second line as soon as its cash covers the cheapest line, so an early contract decides the day. On Inner Boston, seeds 1–12: on target on 8 (seeds 1, 2, 3, 6, 7, 8, 9 and 10: 16.0–24.5 min); **early on 4**, each after a contract on day 9 (seeds 4, 5 and 12 on day 10 at 11.2 min; seed 11 on day 15 at 13.8); none late. At k 1.2 and 1,800× none of the 12 was on target: 11 late (25.3–49.3 min) and seed 12 early (13.3). On the fixture, seeds 1, 5, 7 and 11 draw it on day 10 (11.2 min) after a $60,000 contract on day 9; seeds 2, 3 and 4 at 18.6, 19.2 and 16.0 min. An early second line follows luck the player saw happen, after the first card; a late one was the rule.
  - **What was not taken.** A grant paid from 40 instead of 55 (bronze kept at 55) put seeds 1–3 at 19.7–22.1 min, but the grant's bar is not on the spec's list of tunables, every running company would be paid at least $6,400 a quarter, and the debug line's card would become 6.4× its quarter's running net (about 3.5× now), deepening the finding below that the card is the main income; at 45 every seed waits for a third card (25.3 min). A founding line every 15 minutes put them at 23.7–27.7 (two of three late), doubles the price of a short line's default, and misses its own headway in snow. Contract asks (an easier HEADWAY card) gave 31.7, 31.7 and 20.5: the spread is the asks' luck. 3,600× (an hour a second), by the same arithmetic, would put 6 of the 12 seeds early.
  - **The bands.** The fixture's seed 7 is one of the early ones, so `pacing.test.ts` holds its own milestone, not PLAN §3's: the second line on days 9–13 at 10–14 minutes (measured day 10, 11.2), the report card on day 8 at 9.1, silver on days 18–30 (day 22). PLAN §3's minutes are held on Inner Boston instead: `bench/pacing.ts` exits 1 when profit, the card or the second line misses them, and with them **Boston's balance bands** (EC-12): the founding recipe's day-2 running net $800–$1,400 (measured $1,109 on seeds 1–3), the second line on days 18–35 (28–33), and the first card 60–70 (64.8–65.8). The best first line's day-2 net is held at $1,500–$3,500 by `bench/firstline.ts` (measured $2,523, Washington Street from its first end). The nightly runs both, pacing on seeds 1–3; a failure blocks no merge, so a PR that moves Inner Boston's pace is caught the next morning.
  - **I10, on both cities.** On the fixture (`startingCash.test.ts`): the best first line found without the debug line, Copley to Kenmore whole with one Sparrow ($261,000 with its depot, +$148.87 on day 2), fits the starting cash and turns a daily profit within the quarter; a four-bus line of the starter's shape (a depot, four stops, four Sparrows: $546,000), standing for the sweep's best four-bus line as PLAN §5 words I10, costs **1.1506×** the starting cash, which holds by $270 (so any later cut to a Sparrow's or a stop's price, or a dearer starter, must re-run it); k is inside 1.0–1.8. A two- or three-stop line run every few minutes can carry four Sparrows for less, $538,000 or $542,000 (1.134× and 1.142×): a short line run very often, not the network GD-11's middle game is about. Holding it too would cap k at 1.314, measured on Inner Boston seeds 1–12: 7 on target, 4 early and seed 6 late (day 41, 27.7 min, against day 35 and 24.5 at 1.333), so k stays 1.333. On Inner Boston (`bench/firstline.ts`, nightly, now played for a quarter): 18 of 18 corridor lines fit, 4 turn a day's profit, and the best of them, Washington Street from its first end, costs $356,000; the check picks the best profitable line first and then asks whether it fits. The clause is against cash, as D7 states; with the $150k credit line (D10) a four-bus line is buyable at founding with $78,548 of credit to spare (at k 1.2, $31,200), but it would run the company into the floor's countdown, so the middle game still has to be earned.
  - **What else holds.** The report card's bars, re-measured at k 1.333: ten opening companies grade 54.1 / 58.3 / 66.9, as at k 1.2. - **2026-10-14** (`e2e/copy.spec.ts`, `src/ui/copyRules.test.ts`, `src/ui/copy.test.ts`): **the copy pass** (D25 item 3: "Capitalization is important, make sure everything has proper grammar").
  - **The guard.** `copyRules.ts` asks every heading, button, label, option, column, caption, tooltip and accessible name to start with a capital, a digit or a sign, no word but 2D and 3D in capitals, curly apostrophes and no space before punctuation. `e2e/copy.spec.ts` reads every screen from Home through founding, the demand and speed menus, the depot and draft cards, the line panel, Finance, a stop's card, Saves, a day's close and Contracts; `copy.test.ts` reads every table of words (refusals, set-aside lines, ledger rows, the checklist, speeds, medals, event news).
  - **What it found** on #94's build: 34 problems, among them the headway menus' "off" and "every 10 min", "build 859ca0b", Home's "interbus", the founding card's swatches named "#c0392b" and so on, and the draft card's "every 20 min needs 2 Sparrows". Rewordings beyond the guard: refusals that stopped mid-thought ("The stop is already this kind"), counted letters for characters, or read as a past tense ("Name your company first"); the stop card's QUIET and LOSS ("Quiet:", "Losing money:"); "Most profit at" for "Best net at"; one wording for an unreadable save; the no-GPU card's "has none for it"; the silver medal's sentence; "Starting cash" for "Founding capital", ahead of Finance's Day 0.
  - **Kept:** the undo chip's "Undo: Line 2’s fare". With a verb ("Undo: Change Line 2’s fare") the shell at rest late in a game took 6.09% of 1152×720, over I13's 6%; as it is, 5.93%.

- **2026-10-14** (`e2e/demand.spec.ts`, `e2e/sim.spec.ts`, `e2e/tilt.spec.ts`, `e2e/i13.spec.ts`; `src/render/map/{pick,overlays,tilt}.test.ts`, `src/ui/game/latest.test.ts`, `src/platform/web/prefs.test.ts`, `src/sim/rules/catchment.test.ts`): **the map and 3D items of the owner's initial playtest** (D25 items 3 and 7, but for the copy pass, which is its own PR).
  - **The demand menu.** A 26×26 chip beside the compass opens a menu of two checkboxes, Homes (purple) and Jobs (blue), each with how many people or jobs it stands for, so it is the legend too. Each kind's layer is shown or hidden once per change (RN-04), and the choice is kept in this browser under `interbus.prefs.map` through `src/platform`, apart from every save key. Escape closes the menu and stops there, so an open panel stays open. `data-dots` counts what draws: MapLibre's feature query leaves a hidden layer out.
  - **The dot's card, only on a dot.** A dot's radius is 0.12·√people × 0.5 at zoom 12 to × 2 at zoom 16 (a median home dot of 140 people is 1.8 px at the opening zoom 14), so the pointer picks the dot whose circle holds it, else the nearest edge within 6 px (`pick.ts`, sharing the paint expression's numbers). The card reads the dot's own place and count from the sim's list, not the map's tiles, which round its place: a home dot names its people and the people within a walk, a job dot its jobs and the jobs within a walk (`jobsWithinWalk`, a read-only rule beside `peopleWithinWalk`). The sim is asked once per dot, and only the newest question's answer counts, withdrawn when the pointer leaves the dot; before, a late answer could bring the card back over nothing.
  - **The contract rings** are gone with their layers and source; a festival's ring and the draft's walk rings stay.
  - **The gesture.** MapLibre's own mousedown is claimed for a right-drag, a Ctrl-drag and a double-click's second press (with no tool out), so its pan never runs on them. Moved 3 px (MapLibre's click tolerance), the press drags: up tilts at 0.25° a pixel, down flattens, and in 3D sideways turns at 0.8° a pixel (MapLibre's rotate speed). Held still 500 ms (MapLibre's long-press delay), a double-click flips 2D and 3D. A quick double-click zooms a step as before. Preventing the dblclick that follows a held one would also stop the flip's ease halfway, since MapLibre stops the camera whenever a handler takes an event (its `handler_manager.ts`), so the double-click zoom is switched off from the moment a hold is used until that dblclick has passed.
  - **Turning.** Released, a turn within 7° of north snaps to it (MapLibre's bearingSnap); flattening always faces north; Q and E turn 15° and a second press turns on from where the first is going; touch rotation is on in 3D only. - **2026-10-14** (`npm run bench routes`; `src/sim/world.test.ts`, `src/sim/city/route.test.ts`): **routes without loops in Back Bay and downtown** (D25, the owner's initial playtest: "it makes all these large loops").
  - **Two causes:**
    - **Stops on the highway.** 188 of Inner Boston's 11,687 street nodes touch only motorways or ramps: the Mass Pike under Back Bay and the I-93 tunnel and its ramps downtown. A click near one put the stop on the highway, and its buses drove to the next exit and back. Prudential to Back Bay station came back by 7,976 m for 442 m.
    - **One-way streets kept on the way out only.** A leg had to leave and reach each stop in the street's direction, so a stop on a one-way street facing against the line sent the bus round the block. The way back already drove the way out in reverse, against those streets (`rules/position.ts`).
  - **The fix:**
    - Stops, depots and route ends snap through one grid that leaves those nodes out (`world.ts` `stopNode`; `config/lines.ts` `NO_STOP_CLASSES`). Buses still drive the highways between stops.
    - The router drives every street both ways (`BUSES_KEEP_ONE_WAY`, false). One-way streets stay in the city file for the lane markings (PLAN §8 rule 9).
    - No rebake, and no save byte changes.
  - **Seeded click pairs 250–600 m apart**, the distance driven against the straight line between the nodes they snap to. The four routings are before, stops off motorways, one-way not kept, and both:

    | 500 pairs | Median | p99 | Worst | Over 2× | On a motorway |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | Back Bay, before | 1.49× | 19.1× | 41.3× | 30.1% | 21 |
    | Back Bay, stops off motorways | 1.46× | 5.9× | 7.6× | 28.5% | 0 |
    | **Back Bay, both** | **1.27×** | **2.1×** | **2.4×** | **1.4%** | 0 |
    | Downtown, before | 1.62× | 9.8× | 20.4× | 28.8% | 31 |
    | Downtown, stops off motorways | 1.60× | 5.4× | 9.8× | 24.2% | 0 |
    | **Downtown, both** | **1.27×** | **2.1×** | **2.2×** | **1.6%** | 0 |

  - **Named legs, there and back** (metres, straight line in brackets):
    - Copley to Boylston at Mass Ave: 1,203 / 834 → 826 / 826 (825).
    - Prudential to Back Bay station: 907 / 7,976 → 600 / 600 (422).
    - Downtown Crossing to Government Center: 817 / 945 → 679 / 679 (481).
    - South Station to Park Street: 1,515 / 1,334 → 1,066 / 1,066 (693).
  - **What else moved:**

    - Its running cost fell from $3.18 to $2.91 a departure, its riders rose from 610.5 to 639.7 a day, and its first quarter's card went from 75.6 to 76.1.

    - With one-way streets kept, both Inner Boston boxes fail: 28.5% and 24.2% of pairs over twice the straight line, and the worst over 5 times. The router's both-ways test fails too.

No earlier version had one; V1 had only a lost-context card (`Intralines@7af45b1:src/map/MapView.tsx:124-131`).
  - **The rungs** (`render/map/gpu.ts`, decided once before the map mounts): **full** where WebGL2 runs on a GPU; **Low graphics** where it runs in software, 2D only, the map at one pixel a pixel and the buses' frames asked for at most 30 times a second; **none** without WebGL2, a calm card, "Turn on hardware acceleration to see the map", high on the board so Home stays clear below it, with the game, Saves and Export working as ever. A context the browser takes away mid-game shows a card with Reload, and clears if the browser gives it back. (Since amended, 2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above: both cards say the game is saved only while it saves itself, and offer Export this game otherwise.) Low graphics' `T` says "the map stays flat on this computer" for 4 s, until week 10's chip says why it is off.
  - **Software, known two ways.** WebGL's `failIfMajorPerformanceCaveat` alone missed it: the e2e tests' Chromium (with `--enable-unsafe-swiftshader`) grants WebGL2 on SwiftShader without the caveat, reporting "ANGLE (Google, Vulkan 1.3.0 (SwiftShader Device (Subzero) …), SwiftShader driver)". So a renderer named SwiftShader, llvmpipe, softpipe or Microsoft Basic Render Driver counts as software too.
  - **Tests.** Since CI draws in software, the e2e tests now play in Low graphics, which tests the probe on every run; the tilt tests ask `?gpu=full`, and `?gpu=none` forces the card. `?gpu=` and `?fps` are read through `src/platform/` (`web/switches.ts`); a player on a slow machine can ask for `?gpu=low`. On this container's SwiftShader the map draws about 5 frames a second while buses run, under the cap, so the cap itself is tested as a function (`frameDelayMs`). TS-04: with software taken for hardware, a rung granted without WebGL2, SwiftShader's name unknown or no cap, the matching test fails.

It sits beside the build SHA, outside the schema the hash covers, so it is no new format: an older build reads a newer save and ignores it, a save from before it shows no time, and a time that is not a number makes the save unreadable as any mistyped field does. Saves writes it in the player's own time zone ("saved Oct 13, 20:41", the year only when it is not this one).

- **2026-10-13** (`npm run bench jumps`; `src/sim/rules/transition.test.ts`, `src/worker/i6.test.ts`, `src/sim/settling.test.ts`, `src/sim/save/rebase.test.ts`): **no bus jumps on a change made with buses out** (the M4 spec, item 4; I6).
  - **Before.** Positions are a pure function of a line's day plan, its streets and the clock, so any change rebuilt every bus out at once. At 08:00 on day 2 with the debug line every 10 minutes (`npm run bench jumps`, the host and the fleet the vehicle layer draws, buses paired in order): taking Symphony off moved a bus **262.1 m**, extending beyond Longwood **2,774.4 m**, Metro 40s **94.4 m**, every 15 or every 5 minutes **1,570.3 m**, and roadworks starting at a midnight with the last trips out **189.6 m**.
  - **The rule** (`rules/transition.ts`), one timeline as the spec asks: a command, a new depot, or roadworks starting or ending recompute *in service*, and a line whose plan changed with any of its buses out keeps the version it ran on (its places, model, depot drives and plan numbers; save format 12). Its plan in effect is merged: every trip that left before the change finishes on its own version, route and times; the departures from the change on are the new version's, each taken by the bus free longest at the first stop or one from the depot that can reach it in time, never more than the line owns (one no bus can make is not run). Where the first stop or the model changed, the old buses go home after their trip, setting off no earlier than the change. The merged plan hands over to the line's own day plan at the first moment from which every bus does exactly what one of the day plan's does, so nothing moves there either; the close after it lets the earlier version go. Undo, a load and a rebased save recompute plainly, so an undo puts the game back byte for byte; a rebased save starts each line settled. Since amended (2026-10-15, the third audit round, above): every change in service is merged, with buses out or not; a line just drawn changes from a version that ran no bus and keeps every version until its draw day's close; a bus carries on only if every change since its trip kept its first stop and model; and the close retimes for an event before it lets settled lines go. And again (2026-10-16, the fourth audit round, above): a line keeps every version it ran on the day of a change until that day's close, which lets them all go once it has settled and otherwise trims to the last version it had settled onto.
  - **After.** All six cases move no bus at all (0.0 m). `src/worker/i6.test.ts` drives each through the host and the fleet: no bus moves over 5 m at the change, every bus is within 25 m of where one was a second before for three game hours (leaving or reaching a depot aside), and buses out never exceed buses owned, a swap's old buses, sold at the click, finishing their trips beside the new ones. `transition.test.ts` adds 25 random instants for each of six changes on a synthetic line.
  - **The handover**, at 25 random instants of each change on the debug line laid straight (`transition.test.ts`'s six, no overnight service): **20–30 minutes** after a shorter headway, roadworks, an extension, a dropped first stop or a model swap, but **5 to 17 hours** after a longer headway on the same buses, which keeps a bus spare until service ends (the next morning's first departure at the latest). On lines running overnight, 20 minutes to 18 hours, always inside the plan's two days. Since amended (2026-10-16, the fifth audit round, above): a line parks the buses a change leaves spare as its day plan would, overnight service or not, so after a longer headway it hands over at p50 50 to 54 minutes, at most 387 on `npm run bench parking`'s grid, and never at the window's end, where a line running overnight had handed over after a cut and a bus jumped.
  - **Costs** bill what the buses do: each leg and depot drive at the length, model and depot of the version it ran on. Extending the debug line at 07:45 banks from the change exactly the forecast made at it (`settling.test.ts`); billed on the new version throughout, the same hours would cost $388.62 in running against $384.84.
  - **Cost of it.** Inner Boston's whole recompute: p50 98.4 ms with every line settled, 102.5 ms with all ten corridors settling at once.
  - **Tests** (TS-04): with every change recomputed plainly, all six I6 cases fail at the change; with a bus waiting at the first stop sent home as if it had set off after its last trip, dropping a first stop or swapping the model throws it 182.9 m (1,001 m through the host for Metro 40s); with the handover judged at a departure, the day plan's bus already pulling out for it was missing there; with every hour billed on the new version, the rows miss the forecast by $3.78.
  - **Not done.** A swap's old buses are drawn at the new model's length until they are home, and the line card does not say that a line is settling.
